It controls edit granularity across five levels, defined by the fraction of the object's surface area each edit affects. It constructs multiple valid targets when an instruction leaves the choice of part open. A vision-language model judges each edit from views chosen to expose the edited parts, and human raters audit its judgments. ShapeQuest has 87,824 queries in 50 categories and a 50,919-shape gallery with competing edits. To distinguish these variants, ShapeQuester learns retrieval alongside reconstruction of the edited point cloud, with part masks supervising changed and preserved regions. With a frozen pretrained point encoder, ShapeQuester reaches 50.4 operation-macro mAP@10 on the test split, against 47.2 for the fusion baseline with the same inputs and 48.6 for the strongest baseline. Reconstruction adds 6.5 points on the validation split when the point encoder is trained from scratch, including 3.3 from part masks.
  </details>

- **[VenusOCR2.0: Towards Efficient and Effective Document Parsing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32617)**  `arXiv:2611.32617`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lei Li, Zheng Wei, Yi Yuan, Qingbin Liu, Jiang Bian, Zang Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present VenusOCR2.0, a compact 1B-parameter vision-language recognizer designed for both high-accuracy and high-throughput document parsing across text, tables, and formulas. To continuously improve recognition quality without self-training collapse, we build a bounded recursive self-improvement (RSI) data engine where the model acts strictly as a weakness probe, while external multi-expert medoid consensus, VLM visual verification, and factorized synthesis supply reliable supervision across pretraining, curated SFT, and RLVR. VenusOCR2.0 couples a dynamic-resolution SigLIP2 encoder and 2x2 spatial token merging with a Qwen3-0.6B decoder, adopting compact atomic OTSL tokens to shorten table sequences and reduce structural errors. To further eliminate the autoregressive decoding bottleneck, we introduce PTPv2, a lightweight ($\sim$67M) decoupled parallel draft module that combines frozen multi-layer verifier conditioning, a standalone learned mask, and block-causal all-position next-token prediction. On OmniDocBenchv1.6, VenusOCR2.0 achieves an overall score of 96.45 and a state-of-the-art 98.57 formula CDM, substantially outperforming massive general-purpose VLMs such as Gemini-3-Pro (92.91) and Qwen3-VL-235B (89.78). Deployed in vLLM on a single H20 GPU, PTPv2 delivers a 2.96x single-request speedup and up to 2.36x higher batched throughput while guaranteeing lossless, token-for-token equivalence with standard autoregressive decoding.
  </details>

- **[UnifiedAD: One-Shot Anomaly Detection across Modalities via Evidence Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32551)**  `arXiv:2611.32551`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shangze Li, Chuancheng Shi, Simiao Xie, Wenhua Wu, Xingyu Zhu, Fei Shen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Both single-modal and multi-modal anomaly detection aim to identify deviations from normal patterns, providing a common foundation for unified detection. However, existing methods rely on modality-specific modeling and scoring, making it difficult to obtain comparable anomaly evidence with limited normal references. To that end, we propose UnifiedAD, which unifies single-modal and multi-modal anomaly detection through anomaly evidence calibration, requiring only one normal reference per target category and no gradient updates. Specifically, we first derive neuron-level discriminative priors from normal and anomalous samples in an independent auxiliary dataset and combine them with neuron response consistency under normality-preserving transformations of the target reference to select reliable neurons, termed anomaly neurons, and identify anomaly directions. Next, we construct a low-rank normal subspace for each layer and modality from the selected neurons' spatial responses to the reference and quantify query feature deviations from normal structure using projection residuals. Finally, we separately calibrate projection residuals and anomaly-direction responses using the response distributions obtained from the normal reference and its transformations, converting them into comparable anomaly evidence for aggregation across layers and modalities. Experiments on multiple anomaly detection benchmarks demonstrate that UnifiedAD achieves strong performance in both image-level anomaly detection and pixel-level anomaly localization.
  </details>

- **[RSReasoner: Reformulating and Auditing Geospatial Reasoning Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32536)**  `arXiv:2611.32536`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xu Zhang, Xinpeng Zhang, Yunrui Zou, Hongliang Chen, Xiaotian Jiang, Yang Zheng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Geospatial reasoning segmentation aims to localize geographic targets from language requiring semantic inference beyond direct visual perception. However, existing benchmarks often operationalize reasoning as category omission while overlooking attribute mixing, which neglects explicit-category reasoning, introduces perceptual shortcuts, and fails to precisely evaluate reasoning capability. To diagnose and address these limitations, we first reformulate geospatial reasoning segmentation along category expression (explicit vs. implicit) and attribute nature (perceptual vs. inferential), and audit 90,708 queries across existing benchmarks to reveal that pure explicit-inferential queries account for only 0.61% whereas 31.98% mix both attribute types. We then develop a three-stage pipeline that harnesses OpenStreetMap attributes while mitigating geometric and semantic noise to construct RSReasoner, a large-scale dataset of 106,610 images with 213,220 reasoning queries, and RSReasoner-Bench, a diagnostic benchmark of 75,400 queries across four canonical types and controlled variants. Extensive experiments demonstrate that RSReasoner-Bench precisely evaluates model capability across multiple dimensions while exposing shortcut behaviors, and fine-tuning on RSReasoner boosts both reasoning and cross-type generalization. Our findings offer key insights for future geospatial reasoning research and benchmark design. Datasets and code will be released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[DiffAnchor: Anchor-Space Diffusion for Monocular 3D Hand Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32513)**  `arXiv:2611.32513`  `cs.CV`  
  _Changlong Jiang, Youci Tang, Haohong Kuang, Hang Xu, Jinghong Zheng, Ran Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate and efficient monocular 3D hand pose estimation is an important perception capability for embodied human interaction and dexterous manipulation. Recent diffusion-based methods model pose uncertainty by iteratively denoising 3D joint coordinates. However, directly diffusing the final joints requires the generative process to simultaneously explore plausible poses and recover precise coordinates, often relying on additional denoising steps or multiple hypotheses and consequently increasing inference cost. In this work, we propose DiffAnchor, a few-step anchor-space diffusion framework that diffuses anchors rather than joints. Our key insight is that stochastic exploration does not need to operate directly on the final output. Instead, diffusion searches for informative intermediate anchors that serve as local 3D regressors, while anchor-to-joint offset prediction and weighted aggregation complete precise joint localization. Specifically, fixed context anchors provide stable global references, whereas noisy dynamic anchors are iteratively refined toward informative joint regions. An importance-guided anchor selection and replenishment mechanism further retains useful anchors, removes ineffective ones, and explores insufficiently covered regions. This design reduces the precision burden of the diffusion process and enables accurate prediction using only a few denoising steps and a single hypothesis. Extensive experiments on HO-3D V2, NYU, ICVL, and InterHand2.6M demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on the first three benchmarks and competitive performance on challenging interacting-hand estimation, while maintaining inference efficiency comparable to deterministic regression baselines.
  </details>

- **[Déjà-VAE: Training-Free Consistent Editing with Pseudo-Invertible Encoders and Latent Back-Projection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32501)**  `arXiv:2611.32501`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ron Libman, Yamit Ehrlich, Ohad Fried, Assaf Shocher_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Consistency is essential to generative workflows: repeated processing should preserve information beyond the changes explicitly requested. In latent diffusion models, this requires stability both when moving between latent and pixel spaces and when applying successive edits. Autoencoding errors and unintended changes from the editing model can accumulate across these operations, progressively altering image structure, texture, and color. We introduce Déjà-VAE, a framework that combines exact autoencoding consistency with calibrated editing stabilization, without training or fine-tuning. Its autoencoding component converts a pretrained variational autoencoder into a surjective pseudo-invertible neural network, with a fixed gain balancing deviations from the pretrained encoder and decoder. The modified decoder $D'$ is an explicit nonlinear right inverse of the encoder $E'$, satisfying $E'(D'(z))=z$ by construction. Consequently, $D'$ is a reflexive generalized inverse: latent round trips preserve their input, up to numerical precision, and repeated image reconstruction is idempotent. To suppress editing drift, adaptive wavelet back-projection finds the closest latent to the editor's output in $\ell_2$, subject to matching the previous latent on wavelet coefficients whose changes fall below calibrated thresholds. This correction is applied once per turn, with no additional network evaluations after calibration. Together, these components support workflows that remain in latent space or interleave generation with pixel-space operations and constraints. Tests across models and editing benchmarks show less drift than stock pipelines and dedicated consistency methods.
  </details>

- **[COHERE4D: Training-Free Dense Surface Alignment for Coherent Metric 4D Human-Scene Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32482)**  `arXiv:2611.32482`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhumei Wang, Shaofei Wang, Xiang An, Mingtao Pei, Siyuan Huang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Human and scene foundation models offer complementary strengths for monocular 4D reconstruction: human models recover metric-scale body meshes but lack scene grounding, whereas scene models estimate globally consistent 3D geometry and camera motion without reliable metric scale. Crucially, both models reconstruct the same foreground human surface. Yet existing methods do not directly exploit this shared surface: lacking reliable foreground human geometry in scene reconstructions, earlier approaches either resort to sparse contact constraints or learn data-driven alignment modules that generalize poorly to out-of-distribution scenes. We present COHERE4D, a training-free framework that directly registers the human surfaces predicted by independently pretrained human and scene foundation models to reconstruct a coherent, metric 4D world. COHERE4D establishes dense, pixel-aligned 3D correspondences over the visible human surface and employs RANSAC with cross-frame consensus to suppress outliers from occlusion, background contamination, and depth noise. This formulation solves for two complementary geometric quantities: a sequence-level shared scale that calibrates scene geometry and camera motion to metric human size, and per-frame human translations that align camera-space human meshes with the scaled scene geometry to enforce spatial and temporal consistency. Across diverse backbones and benchmarks, COHERE4D substantially outperforms prior methods in human--scene alignment, metric scene and camera pose reconstruction, and global human motion estimation, adding only 4,ms/frame, enabling overall inference at up to 14 FPS.
  </details>

- **[OmniPersist: Persistent Dynamic World Modeling via Coupled Panoramic Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32473)**  `arXiv:2611.32473`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhilu Zhang, Xiaoyu Liu, Wenxi Liu, Shengfeng He_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Action-conditioned world models play a vital role in robotics and autonomous driving, yet existing video diffusion models struggle to maintain dynamic object persistence. When the camera turns away and returns, out-of-view moving entities fail to evolve plausibly in latent space, often mutating or vanishing entirely due to bounded perspective views. To resolve this issue, we propose OmniPersist, a unified framework that couples perspective video generation with panoramic evolution within a single-stage diffusion model, without relying on auxiliary models or initial panoramic inputs. To maintain efficiency, we model the panoramic stream at a lower frame rate, bridging both views across space and time via Geometric Anchoring (GeoAnch) for spatial correspondence and Temporal Compensation (TemComp) for temporal aggregation. OmniPersist is agnostic to camera-control architectures, supporting both latent-level and attention-level conditioning. Extensive experiments on a newly constructed dynamic benchmark across image-to-video and video continuation tasks demonstrate consistent gains in visual quality, camera controllability, revisit consistency, and dynamic persistence.
  </details>

- **[TDD: Target-Discrepancy Distillation for Underwater Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32467)**  `arXiv:2611.32467`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zefan Feng, Zhaocheng Yu, Laibin Chang, Kui Jiang, Junjun Jiang, Xiaopeng Fan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Underwater degradation changes image appearance and can compromise the semantic cues used to guide enhancement. Identifying scene content alone does not explicitly specify the appearance to recover or the input-dependent changes associated with that target. We propose target-discrepancy knowledge distillation (TDD) for underwater image enhancement, which organizes guidance around these two aspects of the same restoration problem. For each degraded-reference training pair, reference features from an underwater vision-language teacher provide target-appearance supervision, while paired feature residuals describe the observation's discrepancy relative to the same target. Both representations are jointly distilled into a lightweight guidance network that predicts them from the degraded image alone; neither the teacher nor reference images are required at inference. To align their use with their supervisory roles, we introduce progressive knowledge fusion within a multi-scale enhancement network. The estimated discrepancy first conditions spatial feature updates, after which target-appearance guidance refines the updated features and channel interactions consolidate their responses. This correction-before-refinement design lets target-guided refinement build on discrepancy-conditioned features. Experiments on six underwater benchmarks demonstrate competitive performance across full-reference and no-reference metrics. Ablations and feature visualizations analyze the contributions of the knowledge source, joint distillation, and progressive fusion.
  </details>

- **[ViDM: Efficient and Order-Robust Parallel Visual Decision Making](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32461)**  `arXiv:2611.32461`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xudong Tan, Peng Ye, Chenyu Huang, Yaoxin Yang, Huafeng Qin, Tao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual decision-making demands accurate judgments and timely responses. Yet for multiple questions sharing the same visual input, autoregressive multimodal large language models generate answers token by token, accumulating sequential decoding costs as question count grows and delaying completion of the decision set. Their predictions can also vary with question order. We introduce ViDM, which transforms multi-question visual inference into parallel decisions in a single forward pass, improving both accuracy and inference efficiency. ViDM organizes shared visual context, multiple questions, and question-specific decision slots into one sequence, reusing the native language-model head to produce all answer distributions simultaneously. A Decision-Query Mask aligns each decision query's direct attention with shared context and its corresponding question, while case-balanced multi-order training strengthens robustness to question reordering. Across nine benchmarks spanning image, multi-image, and video tasks, ViDM improves Qwen3.5-4B's average accuracy from 73.49% to 77.55% while achieving 8.4x the baseline's average end-to-end throughput. On GQA, where each evaluated image has 11.4 questions on average, the end-to-end speedup reaches 20.3x. ViDM also improves mean and worst-order accuracy while reducing order-induced variation across multiple benchmarks, with further validation on LLaVA-OneVision-7B and InternVL3.5-4B.
  </details>

- **[ObsBridge: Observation-Grounded Conditioning for Ego Trajectory Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32447)**  `arXiv:2611.32447`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhenhao Wang, Shaofeng Liang, Jiemin Wu, Sijia Chen, Keni Shi, Nan Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pedestrian forecasting from ego-view observations is challenging because occlusion, limited visibility, and tracking errors corrupt observed motion histories. Such errors are particularly consequential for autonomous robot navigation. Existing paradigms typically either impute noisy history positions before forecasting or jointly train an auxiliary history reconstruction branch. However, predictors trained on clean histories may be sensitive to residual errors in recovered histories at deployment. Meanwhile, auxiliary reconstruction still computes inter-agent interactions over raw, corrupted coordinates. We investigate conditioning future prediction on both recovered historical states and raw observation evidence, with progressive training to accommodate imperfect recovery. Building on this insight, we propose ObsBridge, a flow-based trajectory forecasting framework with observation-grounded historical bridging. Specifically, ObsBridge first employs a frozen estimator to recover historical motion from agent-relative and ego-relative positions, image-plane locations, and visibility states. During training, we introduce an agent-wise curriculum that gradually replaces clean teacher histories with complete estimated histories before context encoding. This progressively exposes the predictor to estimated histories during training. Furthermore, we design a residual geometry adapter to fuse the encoded motion with original observation evidence. We aggregate the resulting representations through social attention to condition the flow-based trajectory generator. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that ObsBridge outperforms state-of-the-art methods. On EgoTraj-TBD, it reduces minADE@20 by 6.5\% and minFDE@20 by 8.7\%. The code will be provided atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[A Mesh to Remember: Persistent Appearance for Interactive Video Worlds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32445)**  `arXiv:2611.32445`  `cs.CV`  
  _Joonghyuk Shin, Alex Trevithick, Katarína Tóthová, Liyuan Zhu, Shengyu Huang, Jaesik Park, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video world models synthesize photorealistic observations in response to user actions but struggle to remain consistent over long interactions. Coding agents and text-to-3D models instead generate explicit 3D worlds whose geometry and state persist by construction, but whose appearance falls far short of photorealism. Recent methods combine the strengths of both by rendering such worlds into coarse proxies that guide a video model. However, since these proxies only specify where things are, the renderer must invent what they look like as the world is explored, and anything it forgets is reinvented when a surface reappears. Our key insight is that a mesh identifies which surface each pixel shows, across frames and views, even as objects move, allowing it to serve as an address for what the renderer generates. We propose M2R (Mesh to Remember), a few-step causal video renderer that stores its own features on the surfaces they depict, in each object's frame, and retrieves them by rasterizing the mesh whenever those surfaces come back into view. Appearance thus travels with objects wherever they move, persists across revisits, and is shared by every agent that observes it, while memory grows with the surfaces seen instead of the number of frames. Trained only on synthetic data, M2R transfers zero-shot to real indoor scans and to worlds authored by coding agents. It outperforms state-of-the-art methods in consistency across static, dynamic, and multi-agent benchmarks, more than doubling object permanence and nearly doubling cross-agent consistency, while rendering 720p video in real time (40 FPS).
  </details>

- **[TESS: Query-Guided Temporal Event-Structured Frame Selection for Long-Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32410)**  `arXiv:2611.32410`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qi Lu, Shiyan Tong, Shujie Li, Sen Li, Likun shi, Maofei Que, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Temporal localization in long videos is challenging because brief, question-relevant events are dispersed among extensive redundant content. Limited visual budgets increase the risk of omitting these events or their temporal context, undermining reliable reasoning. Existing frame selection methods emphasize semantic relevance or temporal coverage, often overlooking the complementary evidence needed to locate related events and establish their temporal relationships. To address this limitation, we propose TESS, a training-free framework for query-guided temporal event-structured frame selection. TESS selects frames that jointly show the reference event and the event asked about, in the required temporal order. Leveraging frozen vision-language representations, TESS integrates soft temporal gating, role-aware budget allocation, and within-role redundancy suppression to preserve complementary event evidence. Extensive experiments across four long-video understanding benchmarks and three multimodal backbones demonstrate that TESS consistently outperforms the strongest compared baseline by 4.0-5.5 percentage points under a fixed frame budget. Further analyses reveal greater improvements on longer videos and robust performance under tighter visual budgets, while independent evidence annotations confirm enhanced event and temporal-relation coverage. These findings establish explicit temporal evidence organization as an effective training-free strategy for preserving event relationships and supporting efficient temporal reasoning over long videos. Our code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Every Edit Its Own Budget: Per-Instruction Token Freezing for Training-Free Acceleration of Multi-Edit Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32407)**  `arXiv:2611.32407`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jyotirmoy Nath, Neeraj Kumar, Brejesh Lall_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accelerating instruction-based image editing when one instruction requests several edits is challenging. Many training-free accelerators for flow-matching image editors skip tokens whose prediction has stopped changing, but decide this with a single threshold for the whole image. With several edits, such a threshold, tuned for speed, freezes small and slowly settling edits first, and requested edits disappear. We propose TIDE (Training-free Instruction-aware Drift-based Editing), which gives every instruction part its own compute budget. First, TIDE finds the region of each part in the editor's own attention by unbalanced optimal transport, without masks or external models. Second, it freezes a token once its drift is small relative to its own part and moves it along the straight path to its endpoint estimate. Third, a sparse engine computes only the remaining tokens. We prove that part-agnostic rules cannot guarantee per-part error budgets, characterize the optimal per-part threshold policy up to one tie class per part, and derive when our rule attains it. On FLUX.2 Klein, one global threshold breaks at least one edit's budget in 89% of MICE-Bench images, and TIDE drops up to 77% fewer edits at matched or higher speed (48% on MICE-Bench), a gap human raters confirm. On the many-step Klein Base, TIDE is the fastest compared method on six benchmarks (4.81-4.89x) and needs 5.3x fewer TFLOPs than vanilla, the largest saving we measure. With the same settings, it is also the fastest compared method on Qwen-Image-Edit-2511, a second model family (3.46-3.52x), and drops the fewest edits on all six benchmarks. Code will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[Improving Character Identity Consistency in Diffusion-Based Visual Story Generation through Identity Memory and Automatic Reranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32383)**  `arXiv:2611.32383`  `cs.CV`  
  _Maria-Emilia Grigore, Stelian Spînu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automatic visual story generation using diffusion-based models has become an increasingly important research direction in generative artificial intelligence. However, maintaining character identity consistency across consecutive frames remains an open challenge, as existing models often produce significant variations in facial traits, clothing, visual style, or anatomical proportions. This paper proposes a modular pipeline to improve character identity consistency in multi-frame visual story generation using Stable Diffusion XL alongside additional conditioning and automatic selection mechanisms. The proposed system integrates LoRA fine-tuning for character personalization, ControlNet for pose-based structural control, and IP-Adapter for visual identity transfer. Furthermore, a reranking mechanism based on Best-of-K candidate selection and persistent identity memory is introduced to automatically select the most visually consistent generated images. The work also includes integrating and extending the StoryDiffusion framework into a reproducible local workflow, along with improvements for generated frame selection. Performance evaluation uses automatic metrics such as CLIP image similarity, DINO similarity, and CLIP text-image alignment. Experimental evaluation was conducted on a custom benchmark of multiple characters and visual stories organized into difficulty levels (easy, medium, and difficult). Both progressive variants of the proposed pipeline and extended versions of StoryDiffusion were evaluated, including SDXL baseline, Best-of-K, Identity Memory, and the Full Pipeline. Results show that reranking, identity memory, and visual conditioning reduce identity drift across frames and improve the overall coherence and stability of generated visual stories.
  </details>

- **[Visual-State Graph Memory for Streaming Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32369)**  `arXiv:2611.32369`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ailar Mahdizadeh, Pritam Sarkar, Xiangteng He, Leonid Sigal_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In streaming video understanding, the evidence needed to answer a question may have already left the recent-frame window. Keeping all frames does not scale with video length, while dropping older frames limits the model's ability to reason about the past. We propose Visual-State Graph (VSG), a training-free memory that stores the state of a stream rather than the raw sequence of frames. VSG represents the stream as a graph of objects and their relations, together with a log of events; similar to a dynamically evolving scene graph. An object observed repeatedly is represented by one persistent node, with a timestamped history of how its state changes. To determine whether a newly mentioned object already exists in the graph, we compare the relations it takes part in with those of stored objects. We build this memory as the video arrives, before the questions are known. When a question arrives, we retrieve relevant objects and events alongside recent frames under a fixed input budget. Our evaluation spans four streaming-video benchmarks and seven open-source vision-language models ranging from 7B to 32B parameters. Under matched input budgets, VSG improves reasoning about the past while preserving real-time understanding.
  </details>

- **[LookAnything: A Foundation Model for Holistic Gaze Following](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32351)**  `arXiv:2611.32351`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiayu Tang, Yuchen Zhou, Hongsheng Wu, Hanjing Ye, Ke Huang, Cheng Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Human gaze provides a rich window into attention and intent, making its understanding essential for applications ranging from social signal analysis to human-robot interaction. Existing methods typically employ highly customized architectures relying on auxiliary inputs like head crops or depth maps, causing cascading errors and inference overhead. Trained on single domains and confined to isolated gaze following with heatmap outputs, they lack flexibility and generalization for diverse interactive scenarios. To bridge this gap, we present LookAnything, a foundation model that unifies diverse gaze-centric tasks for holistic gaze following. Benefiting from large-scale multi-task and cross-domain training, LookAnything flexibly adapts to various settings, generating diverse outputs (points, boxes, heatmaps, and language) and supporting broad gaze-centric localization, description, and reasoning. At its core, we introduce learnable gaze-prior queries to promote gaze-aware representation learning, eliminating the need for auxiliary external inputs. To facilitate this paradigm, we construct HoliGaze, a comprehensive corpus and benchmark comprising 311K question-answer pairs across 5 capability dimensions and 9 distinct tasks. Experiments demonstrate that LookAnything consistently outperforms 33 leading vision-language models on holistic gaze following, while remaining highly competitive with dedicated specialists across 5 conventional target detection benchmarks. Evaluation on unseen datasets and deployment on a real robot further validate its strong out-of-domain generalization and flexible adaptation in real-world scenarios.
  </details>

- **[LRQ-Cache: Compressing Feature Caches in Video Diffusion via Low-Rank Approximation and Quantization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32345)**  `arXiv:2611.32345`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruizhe Yao, Zhen Huang, Songpan Gao, Danyi Liu, Zijian Cao, Weijie Zheng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video diffusion transformers (DiTs) achieve high-fidelity generation, but inference is expensive because every denoising step evaluates a large transformer over all spatiotemporal tokens of the video. Temporal caching accelerates them by reusing intermediate features across denoising steps. However, retaining dense feature tensors introduces a persistent storage cost that grows with video length, resolution, and cache coverage, reaching 109-346 GiB per video for the predictive caches we evaluate. Such states exceed the memory of a single GPU, so native caches must either be offloaded to the CPU, which slows generation, or be sharded across several GPUs, e.g., with sequence parallelism, so that the cache rather than the compute sets the number of GPUs. We instead shrink the cached state itself, exploiting its redundancy: a few channel patterns shared across tokens capture much of the energy of each cached feature. Based on this observation, we propose LRQ-Cache, a training-free method that combines low-rank approximation and quantization for feature-cache compression. LRQ-Cache represents each cached feature with a small set of factors, quantizes them before storage, and reconstructs the feature at reuse time, leaving cache mechanisms unchanged. Across three caching mechanisms on HunyuanVideo and Wan2.1, LRQ-Cache reduces the persistent state of predictive caches by 42-71x, to 2.3-4.9 GiB per video. VBench stays within 0.25 pp of the native cache for TeaCache, and the larger drops of the Wan2.1 predictive caches, up to 3.5 pp, shrink at higher ranks. As a result, predictive caches that exceed the memory of a single 80 GB H100 GPU fit on it with LRQ-Cache, making generation 2.7-5.9x faster than with CPU-offloaded native caches.
  </details>

- **[Get the Next Moment Right: Short-Term Event Planning for Physically Plausible Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32337)**  `arXiv:2611.32337`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Liu, Xiang Tang, Ruotong Li, Xiaopeng Fan, Yujiu Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video generation models synthesize realistic imagery yet still struggle to produce physically plausible evolution. Existing methods guide generation with a motion trajectory predicted for the entire clip in advance, so prediction errors accumulate and degrade later frames. We propose STEP(Short-Term Event Planning), a training-free closed-loop framework that guides generation one short clip at a time. Each round consists of four steps: grounding measures the scene state from the latest frames, event planning predicts the next physical event and renders it into a control clip, latent-guided generation produces the next short clip under this control, and verification checks whether the event occurs and resamples the clip if it does not. Because each round is grounded in the frames actually generated, control relies only on reliable short-range predictions. STEP improves the physical plausibility of three frozen generators on Physics-IQ Verified and PhyGenBench, and can be applied to new generators without training.
  </details>

- **[Towards a Multimodal Aesthetic Agent for Photographic Reference Grading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32299)**  `arXiv:2611.32299`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kunjie Lin, Haitao Zhang, Bosheng Chen, Lanhui Xu, Gaofeng Pan, Yuxin Lin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reference-guided photographic color grading helps users realize their aesthetic intent by selectively drawing on the color and tonal characteristics of a reference photograph. Doing so requires understanding which characteristics users wish to adopt and translating these preferences into controllable edits. However, image-only reference methods align overall appearance without instruction-grounded selection, while pixel-space generative editors typically produce flattened images without editable parameters, limiting subsequent refinement. To address these challenges, we introduce JarvisAura, a multimodal aesthetic agent that formulates photographic color grading as structured decision-making in an editable parameter space. JarvisAura offers three key advantages: (1) an aesthetic agent paradigm that integrates visual attribute perception, strategy-level reasoning, and parameterized execution for interpretable and controllable photographic color grading; (2) a unified reasoning-to-execution pipeline that connects aesthetic planning with backend rendering and editable parameter refinement; and (3) a three-stage training strategy comprising Parameter-Grounded Continual Pre-Training for parameter-appearance grounding, Reference-Grounded Supervised Fine-Tuning for reference-guided reasoning and parameter decisions, and Reference-Aligned Group Relative Policy Optimization for rendered-outcome refinement with multidimensional rewards and dynamic data derivation. On Ref-Bench, JarvisAura achieves the best scores among methods with results across reported automatic metrics and a 74% human-rated task-success rate, exceeding GPT-Image-2.5 by 9 percentage points.
  </details>

- **[SofaSpace: A Benchmark for Executable Visual Spatial Reasoning in Continuous Motion and Environment Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32285)**  `arXiv:2611.32285`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ze Rong, Kun Shi, Zhedong Zheng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A rigid body can fit at its start and goal yet collide with a wall as it moves between them. Inspired by the moving sofa problem, we introduce SofaSpace, a benchmark for visual spatial reasoning through continuous planar rigid-body transport and environment construction. SofaSpace contains 3,000 scenes organized around three challenges: geometric compatibility, nonlocal maneuver planning, and interactive and temporal coordination. Four task views ask models to plan a motion, reconstruct a layout, design an environment that meets transport requirements, or jointly generate a layout and a motion. A separate set of 534 SofaSpace-Hard tasks tests planning and judgment. A geometric verifier checks the entire body throughout each submitted motion. On static planning tasks, endpoint agreement and continuous validity differ substantially within the same submitted plans: Claude drops from 97.2% to 52.8%, DeepSeek from 100.0% to 30.6%, and Kimi from 97.2% to 29.4%, while the strongest configuration retains 98.3% full validity. Environment construction reveals a further gap. Of 295 feasible layouts generated by the strongest configuration, 181 satisfy all evaluated functional requirements; for Kimi, one of 172 does. These results show how executable checks distinguish endpoint agreement from valid motion and geometric feasibility from functional compliance.
  </details>

- **[StreamCoach: Proactive Sports Coaching via Continuous Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32281)**  `arXiv:2611.32281`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiaoye Liang, Yiming Huang, Jialu Wu, Yushe Cao, Ruxiang Duan, Yi Sun, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In fitness and sports training, timely action quality assessment (AQA) is essential for learners to identify and correct technical errors during movement execution. However, most AQA methods assess completed performances, limiting their ability to provide timely feedback. Streaming methods alleviate this latency, but limited reasoning hinders their use of subtle movement cues and sport-specific knowledge. Moreover, their fragmented feedback focuses on one body part at a time, overlooking movement details of other body parts and transitions between action phases. To address these limitations, we propose StreamCoach, a streaming AQA framework under the OnCueCoaching paradigm that couples continuous chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning with proactive, evidence-gated coaching. Technique-Aware Reasoning tracks body-part movements and action-phase transitions against sport-specific criteria, while Holistic Coaching integrates accumulated evidence to deliver timely, comprehensive feedback on strengths and technical errors. To support training and benchmarking, we construct EE4D-Stream from 1,213 sports video sequences in Ego-Exo4D through LLM-assisted reannotation and manual review of selected claims, pairing dense CoT annotations with evidence-gated speech content. Building on this dataset, we train StreamCoach using Temporal-Semantic Alignment Reinforcement Learning to jointly optimize feedback timing and content. We benchmark 11 offline and streaming baselines on EE4D-Stream, including three proprietary models. StreamCoach achieves state-of-the-art results across intervention timing, action assessment, and coaching quality, surpassing both GPT-6.1 Sol and Claude Opus 5.5.
  </details>

- **[CaveBench: Benchmarking Abductive Physical Reasoning from Internal Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32251)**  `arXiv:2611.32251`  `cs.CV`  
  _Guanxin Lin, Chen Yang, Shenxiang Zeng, Honghui Zhu, Xin Li, Wenbo Deng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) are advancing in physical reasoning, but whether they can understand external physical drivers from internal observations remains an open question. Existing benchmarks have not specifically targeted this setting. To this end, we introduce CaveBench, a benchmark for abductive physical reasoning from internal observations. Models receive apparatus descriptions and internal images while the external controls remain hidden, and must infer the operations responsible for the observed responses. CaveBench spans 20 apparatus scenes in five physical domains and contains 10,200 simulated episodes, split into 9,000 training, 1,000 validation, and 200 test episodes. Questions span one to six events and require the complete control set at every event, testing whether an explanation accounts for the entire process. Synchronized observations and control records provide the inputs and reference answers. Evaluating 27 VLM configurations yields six-event exact-match accuracies of 68.0% for the strongest closed-source model and 12.5% for the strongest open-weight model. Seven adaptation baselines show that single-event gains need not improve complete sequences. Error analysis identifies failures in visual identification, physical inference, evidence integration, and action composition, specifying where current models struggle to connect internal phenomena with their external causes.
  </details>

- **[Can You Find the Edit When Nothing Looks Wrong? A Paired Benchmark for Text-Driven Image Manipulation Localization Beyond Semantic Abnormality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32242)**  `arXiv:2611.32242`  `cs.CV`  
  _Peiyu Zhuang, Jianquan Yang, Zhuoying Cai, Yi Jiang, Yingquan Chen, Xinle Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Instruction-driven image editing can alter a photograph while keeping the result coherent with the scene. Existing benchmarks lack matched plausible and anomalous edits of the same target, making it difficult to measure how localization performance changes with semantic plausibility. We construct a paired benchmark by generating semantically plausible and anomalous replacements of the same target in each source photograph. Both edits use the same editor, language, and generation settings. We estimate a separate mask for each output and retain pairs with sufficient mask overlap, enabling direct comparison of localization performance under the two semantic conditions. The release contains 47,747 bilingual triplets from 6,500 photographs, spanning four editors, three source domains, and six intended violation types. Across nine public localizers, editor-averaged IoU is lower on plausible edits in 26 of 27 model-dataset comparisons. This gap persists under pixel-change controls and region-size analyses. These findings reveal a semantic-plausibility shortcut: localization often weakens when an edited result looks ordinary. The benchmark provides a controlled setting for measuring this gap and developing localizers that remain effective on plausible edits.
  </details>

- **[EvoSearch: Self-Evolving Active Perception for Wide-Area Geospatial Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32214)**  `arXiv:2611.32214`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhenghao Xie, Jing Xiao, Zihao Yu, Wanyi Yu, Liang Liao, Gui-Song Xia, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Geospatial search is fundamental to resource surveys and environmental monitoring, requiring the discovery of relevant evidence across large geographic areas and multiple spatial scales. Existing active perception methods adaptively select observation locations and scales to improve search efficiency, yet their search trajectories become increasingly long and costly as the search area expands. A key limitation is that final-answer correctness alone provides little guidance on which observations to retain and when to stop, hindering efficient wide-area search. To address this challenge, we propose EvoSearch, a self-evolving active perception framework that co-evolves the perception policy and exploration program to compress search trajectories while preserving answer quality. Specifically, quality-cost comparisons between completed branches from shared evidence train the policy to acquire useful observations, avoid redundant ones, and stop when sufficient evidence is available. Execution feedback from the updated policy then guides exploration-program revision, adapting experience generation to the policy's changing observation needs. We further introduce GeoSearchBench, a benchmark for evaluating search quality and visual processing efficiency through relational search and region search. Experiments demonstrate that EvoSearch shortens search trajectories and substantially reduces visual processing while maintaining overall search quality, supporting scalable active perception for wide-area geospatial search.
  </details>

- **[MoGe-4D: Efficient, Flexible, and Unified Dense 4D Reconstruction and Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32198)**  `arXiv:2611.32198`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruicheng Wang, Linlin Xin, Sicheng Xu, Jianfeng Xiang, Zelong Lv, Yu Deng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing dynamic scenes from RGB video requires reasoning about both 3D geometry and motion across viewpoints, yet existing methods either split reconstruction and tracking into fragmented pipelines or face prohibitive dense spatiotemporal decoding costs. We introduce MoGe-4D, a feed-forward framework that unifies geometry and motion prediction via ordered queries of paired allocentric and egocentric dynamic point maps. Each query maps source pixels to a target time simultaneously in both camera frames, resolving relative camera pose in closed form while flexibly serving depth, dynamic registration, and dense 3D tracking. For efficient dense querying, MoGe-4D decouples scene modeling from query decoding: a high-capacity context encoder caches coarse global spatiotemporal features once, while a lightweight query encoder rapidly decodes dense point maps at flexible resolutions. Furthermore, as supervising dense 4D queries demands continuous 3D correspondences whose raw trajectory storage is prohibitive, we introduce RigIDTrack, a synthetic data pipeline and dataset that compresses surface dynamics into rigidity-regularized affine bodies, recovering exact arbitrary-frame 3D trajectories with $\sim15\%$ storage overhead over RGB-D video. Across diverse benchmarks, MoGe-4D achieves state-of-the-art dense 3D tracking, matches dedicated 3D foundation models in reconstruction, and runs substantially faster than prior 4D baselines.
  </details>

- **[What to Change, What to Keep: Experience-Evolving Agents for Video Recasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32196)**  `arXiv:2611.32196`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shiying Zhang, Yuxia Zhu, Huaze Xue, Shengqi Dang, Yizhen Wu, Yijie Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video recasting aims to transform the entities and environments of existing videos into new visual worlds while preserving their essential actions and interactions. However, achieving coherent transformations remains challenging, as coordinated edits can disrupt the original event structure. In this paper, we introduce RecastAgent, an experience-guided agentic framework for source-conditioned video recasting that operates under frozen model weights. Our framework employs an Experience-Guided Planner to choose edit prompts and input video states under qualitative visual critique, allowing the agent to build upon partial progress or backtrack to preserve source structure. Across tasks, an Experience Accumulation module distills completed trajectories into conditional, evidence-linked principles that record both an editing strategy and its scope of validity. We additionally construct a benchmark of 67 source videos with 134 paired-specification tasks across six creative categories for evaluation. Experiments demonstrate that RecastAgent outperforms single-pass and agentic rewriting baselines across Bernini and Ditto. Furthermore, accumulated experience improves performance on held-out test videos and transfers zero-shot to external benchmarks like FiVE-Bench and VE-Bench.
  </details>

- **[MediaStormBench-CameraLog: Can Multimodal Models Identify a Camera's Log Curve?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32172)**  `arXiv:2611.32172`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ming Ma, Wenhao Hu, Qichao Wang, Yuhao Wang, Junpeng Zhao, Qiyi Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  MediaStormBench is a multimodal benchmark for real-world film and television production, with each subset drawn from MediaStorm's day-to-day work. We release its first subset, MediaStormBench-CameraLog (CameraLogBench), on a common foundational task in footage ingest and initial color grading: identifying the Log curve of a clip without metadata. Cameras record Log footage by compressing a wide range of scene light into code values with a vendor-defined logarithmic curve, and color can be restored correctly only once that curve is known. The curve, however, is recorded only in file metadata, easily lost in transcoding and team hand-offs. A colorist then has to try candidate curves one by one against a waveform scope, and a wrong choice shifts the tones and colors of the whole clip. Recovering the curve from the image alone is challenging, because vendor curves cluster tightly in code value and are coupled with unknown exposure. CameraLogBench is built on professionally shot footage and tests whether multimodal models can identify the curve, with a probe that removes the correct option to expose guessing from priors. Because every candidate curve is strictly defined by a published vendor formula, we derive four controls from one implementation of these formulas: graded negatives, synthetic frames of identical scenes, in-context specification digests, and a training-free formula baseline, which together decouple semantic priors from photometric measurement. Across 11 models, real-footage accuracy barely exceeds always guessing the most frequent curve; when the correct option is removed, models almost never abstain, revealing heavy reliance on scene and brand priors. Supplying the vendor specification sharply improves recognition on calibrated charts but hardly helps on natural images without a photometric reference, exposing a gap between reading a specification and measuring pixels.
  </details>

- **[SoccerNarrate: Event-Grounded Streaming Soccer Commentary with Macro-Window Preference Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32153)**  `arXiv:2611.32153`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zihan Jia, Zhilin Dai, Zhengming Zhang, Min Yang, Zhenpeng Huang, Jiaqi Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing clip-based soccer commentary models often produce delayed, repeated, or poorly synchronized narration on untrimmed full-match videos. Recent streaming video-language models make low-latency full-match narration feasible, but they often favor general descriptions rather than professional, event-grounded soccer commentary. They may describe nearby actions while failing to mention key events such as goals, cards, substitutions, and offsides correctly and on time. In this paper, we present SoccerNarrate to bridge low-latency streaming narration with event-grounded professional soccer commentary across data, training, and evaluation. First, we construct SoccerNarrate-Data, a large-scale time-anchored dataset with word-level ASR alignment, quality filtering, and entity calibration. Second, we perform macro-window preference alignment (MWPA), which aligns the model with complete event-level commentary semantics by comparing causal multi-step rollouts from the same streaming prefix while keeping second-level inference unchanged. We further use event-mismatched human commentaries as counterfactual negatives to emphasize event semantics over commentary style. Third, we introduce SoccerNarrate-Eval, an event-centric benchmark based on temporally constrained event entailment. Full-match experiments show that SoccerNarrate improves event coverage and event-aligned precision over strong offline and streaming baselines. Code and models are released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[LiteSTAR: Low-Rank Channel Modulation for Temporally Consistent CVD Video Compensation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32140)**  `arXiv:2611.32140`  `cs.CV`  
  _Peiyang Lin, Sichen Lin, Yipeng Liao, Liqun Lin, Xu Wang, Tiesong Zhao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Color Vision Deficiency (CVD) affects a large population worldwide, yet practical recoloring must run continuously on-device. Frame-wise video recoloring produces color flickering, while existing methods rely on costly cross-frame alignment in pixel or feature space. We observe that color flickering correlates with variations in channel-wise responses, with color-sensitive variations concentrated in a low-rank subspace. Based on this observation, we propose Lightweight Spatio-Temporal Adaptive Recoloring (LiteSTAR), built around LiCaD, a plug-and-play Lightweight Channel-Adaptive Decoder. LiCaD performs temporal filtering in a low-rank state space with learned process and observation uncertainties. It then modulates feature channels to achieve temporal consistency without explicit spatial alignment. LiteSTAR further employs adaptive patch routing to improve color discrimination for CVD users while avoiding unnecessary over-enhancement. We also introduce EgoCVDSet, the first egocentric video benchmark for CVD recoloring. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that LiteSTAR achieves superior performance with only 0.05M parameters. LiCaD further demonstrates strong generalization across different tasks and network architectures. The code, models, and dataset will be released after review.
  </details>

- **[EditText: Structure-Aware Generation and Selective Visual Transport for Fine-Grained Text Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32134)**  `arXiv:2611.32134`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhaobin Chu, Guandong Li, Xin Lv, Zhiwei Zeng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Fine-grained text editing aims to accurately replace text in a specified region while preserving the source text's font, color, and texture, as well as visual consistency in unedited regions. We present EditText, a unified framework for fine-grained text editing that combines target character generation with visual transport from the source image. The model uses dual-condition Fill inputs: a masked image and a source text style reference, both sharing a spatial reference with the target image. The masked image hides the original text and retains unedited content as a source canvas; the style reference supplies appearance cues that guide new character generation together with the target text. We introduce an Attention Transport framework in which Source Weight describes the attention allocated to a condition and the Spatial Aggregation Kernel describes its internal spatial attention distribution. Accurate transfer of source details depends on attention strength, the retention of details in condition features, and spatially accurate aggregation. For selected non-edit queries, we therefore preserve the total attention weight assigned to the masked image while redistributing its attention toward spatially corresponding neighborhoods, enabling selective visual transport. Multi-stage training, combined with a Glyph-aware Structural Loss on local target character structure, teaches the model to generate accurate text, preserve source text appearance, and retain unedited details. We also introduce TBench, a fine-grained text editing benchmark covering Alphabetic, Logographic, and Arabic numerals, to evaluate character updates, appearance preservation, and the preservation of unedited details. On TBench, EditText achieves state-of-the-art non-edit-region SSIM (0.9769) and full-image LPIPS (0.0460).
  </details>

- **[RISE: Reasoning with Implicit State Embeddings for GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32102)**  `arXiv:2611.32102`  `cs.CV`  
  _Enqi Liu, Zhi Gao, Liu Liu, Lifeng Fan, Liyuan Pan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Graphical user interface (GUI) agents translate user instructions and screenshots into executable actions. To learn executable actions, existing approaches often rely on explicit reasoning during training, which encourage over-reliance on specific reasoning patterns rather than generalizable action-relevant information. In this paper, we propose an implicit framework, RISE, which integrates action-relevant reasoning into implicit state representations to guide GUI action prediction. To this end, action-relevant reasoning is captured through three complementary supervision objectives: full-CoT state alignment preserves information across the reasoning process, action-distribution alignment encodes action preferences, and reasoning-to-action boundary alignment captures the representation immediately before action generation. In this case, our RISE effectively learns informative implicit representations, thereby improving the accuracy of next-action token prediction. RISE achieves 70.6\% accuracy on OSWorld-G, establishing state-of-the-art performance among compared 8B models with a 4.3-point gain over its backbone. Results across two distinct backbones and six diverse benchmarks further demonstrate its effectiveness in GUI grounding and action prediction. Our code is available on our project page.
  </details>

- **[SegPFN: Training-Free In-Context Segmentation with Tabular Prior-Data Fitted Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32045)**  `arXiv:2611.32045`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jiajian Ma, Yihao Qiao, Ke Chen, Kaiyue Feng, Wushuang Rui, Abhishek Jha, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In-context segmentation (ICS) uses one or a few annotated reference images to specify what to segment in a target image. Existing training-free ICS approaches typically rely on manually designed inference procedures, which may limit how flexibly reference information is combined. Learned in-context inference offers an adaptive alternative, but acquiring this capability through segmentation-specific training can compromise generalization to unseen domains. We therefore ask: can a pretrained in-context learner infer what to segment from annotated references without segmentation-specific training? We introduce SegPFN, a training-free framework that reformulates ICS as tabular classification over frozen visual features. A frozen tabular prior-data fitted network (PFN) uses labeled reference patches as context to classify patches in the target image. We comprehensively benchmark SegPFN against major existing ICS methods across 11 datasets spanning semantic, part, and personalized segmentation. SegPFN improves overall mean intersection-over-union (mIoU) by 4.7 points over the strongest overall baseline and achieves the highest average mIoU in four of five task-shot settings. Further analyses show that hard-negative reference patches improve fine-grained target discrimination, while joint conditioning enables SegPFN to use multiple references effectively. These results show that SegPFN offers a simple and effective paradigm for training-free in-context segmentation.
  </details>

- **[DynaCue: Comparing Motion and Timing Between Groups of Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32014)**  `arXiv:2611.32014`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A score that distinguishes videos with different temporal-consistency ratings can miss differences associated with motion preference. VideoScore's temporal consistency score distinguished higher- from lower-rated Pika videos in 95% of comparisons with 15 videos per group, yet could not detect the difference between preferred videos and their same-prompt alternatives in any of the 200 comparisons of 41 VideoGen-RewardBench pairs. We introduce DynaCue, a two-sample permutation test that combines complementary measurements of motion, frame continuity, and appearance to compare groups of videos without choosing a single cue in advance. DynaCue measures local changes in optical flow both before and after accounting for each clip's usual activity, captures interruptions through frame differences and freeze counts, and incorporates scores for motion and appearance. In controlled experiments, DynaCue detected group differences across five interventions affecting playback continuity, displacement, or rhythm. In real-world evaluations of generated videos where viewers rated temporal consistency or chose which of two videos they preferred for its motion, DynaCue detected group differences in at least 80% of resampled comparisons on each of the three datasets at the smaller sample sizes studied. Every other method evaluated on all three had a detection rate of 60% or less on at least one dataset.
  </details>

- **[Describe, Don't Decide: Correcting Answer Bias in Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31988)**  `arXiv:2611.31988`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuwei Wu, Longin Jan Latecki_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Comparing two entities in an image, multimodal LLMs (MLLMs) often answer wrongly. We find that the answer bias enters not when the model sees, but when it decides with the image in view: the model's own descriptions of the two entities often already hold the right answer. We therefore propose D$^3$ (Describe, Don't Decide): the model describes each entity alone, decides from these descriptions without the image, and merges this decision with its direct answer. Without any training, D$^3$ improves all 79 model-benchmark pairs across ten benchmarks and eight 7-9B MLLMs. On held-out GQA comparison questions, the mean accuracy of these models rises from 72.4% to 83.2% (up to +21.5% per model), higher than that of Qwen2.5-VL-32B answering directly (82.8%). Code will be available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[InCue: Making Better Use of What Multimodal LLMs Already See at Test Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31987)**  `arXiv:2611.31987`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuwei Wu, Siyue Yu, Longin Jan Latecki_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Even when multimodal large language models (MLLMs) answer incorrectly, their internal cues often point to the relevant region. A widely used remedy re-presents the cue-selected region as an enlarged crop to supply finer detail. However, we find that much of its gain requires no new detail: it largely survives when the crop comes from the already-seen low-resolution input, and partly even when the region's cached tokens are merely copied. Part of re-presentation's gain thus comes from drawing attention to evidence the model already holds. We propose InCue, a test-time framework that makes better use of this evidence without additional visual input. InCue treats visual attention and predictive-entropy gradients as Internal Cues, couples them into a spatial target, and optimizes only an offset on the existing visual tokens to steer attention toward it. Under matched visual-token budgets, InCue achieves state-of-the-art performance, consistently outperforming recent test-time methods and improving four MLLMs by up to 8.9% on V$^*$ and 23.4% on its hard samples. Its gains carry over to eight general benchmarks. Code will be available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[CodeHOI: Code with Agents for Physically Plausible 4D Hand-Object Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31976)**  `arXiv:2611.31976`  `cs.CV`  
  _Seonghwan Park, Inho Chang, Min-Gyu Park_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modeling 4D hand-object interaction (HOI) is fundamental to understanding manipulation. This requires physics, since hands move objects only by touch and each material responds differently. Yet existing methods ignore this physics or simulate only a narrow range of matter. Programs can instead describe any matter with its physics, but coding agents omit the hand that drives the interaction. We close this gap with CodeHOI, to our knowledge the first agentic framework that writes physically plausible 4D HOI as programs. To write one, an Object Agent first builds each object with the physics of its matter. An HOI Agent then specifies the hands by their contacts and simulates each kind of matter with an established physics solver. Finally, an Evaluator Agent checks each result against physical laws. We also introduce CodeHOI-Bench with 100 reconstruction and generation tasks across four kinds of matter. With off-the-shelf frontier models, CodeHOI writes interactions that are plausible for every kind of matter and remain so when transferred to an independent physics engine. It thereby enables coding agents to write physically plausible HOI across diverse matter.
  </details>

- **[SPAR: Self-Referenced World-Model Rewards for Physical Alignment in Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31958)**  `arXiv:2611.31958`  `cs.CV`  
  _Junyi Zhang, Qichao Wang, Wenzhe Qian, Yuanchuan Wang, Min Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generating videos with physically plausible dynamics remains challenging despite advances in visual quality. Latent world models offer predictive signals for evaluating physical plausibility, but higher agreement between predicted and observed features may reflect easier-to-predict scene content rather than more plausible dynamics. We propose \textbf{S}elf-Referenced \textbf{P}hysical \textbf{A}lignment \textbf{R}ewards (\textbf{SPAR}), a framework that uses world-model predictions for inference-time physical alignment. SPAR compares each video's prediction error with that of static clips constructed by repeating its own frames, providing a content-matched reference for evaluating generated dynamics. To account for the uneven spatial and temporal distribution of physical interactions, we introduce a unified \textbf{spatiotemporal reward refinement} strategy. \textbf{Spatial refinement} emphasizes localized prediction discrepancies, while \textbf{temporal refinement} moderates brief error peaks and supplements prediction errors with agreement between predicted and observed feature changes. Evaluations across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that SPAR improves the physical plausibility of both \textbf{text-to-video generation} and \textbf{image-to-video continuation}, bringing generated dynamics into closer alignment with the physical regularities governing real-world events. Additional results and resources are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Open-OmniCoach: Evidence-Enhanced Trajectory Learning for Omni Video Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31891)**  `arXiv:2611.31891`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weisong Liu, Xiangtai Li, Haochen Wang, Haiquan Wen, Zhaoxiang Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Training capable omni video agents requires more than diverse question-answer pairs: agents must learn to reliably find, connect, and correct task-relevant evidence in omni-setting. However, we argue that existing data pipelines provide limited control over task-relevant evidence and weak supervision for these capabilities. In this work, we present Open OmniCoach, a new framework for evidence-seeking omni video agents. It contains a high-quality dataset, OmniCoach-240K, and a new agentic reinforcement learning (RL) method, Step-Level Evidence-Guided Policy Optimization (SEPO). For the former, OmniCoach-240K comprises 248.2K interaction trajectories, including 230.7K multi-span trajectories and 17.4K reflection trajectories. We design a three-stage data pipeline by leveraging both fine-grained omni-evidence and the agent's own errors. Meanwhile, compared with other RL baselines, SEPO provides turn-level credit based on improvements in evidence coverage and temporal localization. Applying both trajectory data and the SEPO method, we obtain Open OmniAgent models. The resulting models outperform the baselines across multiple video and audio-visual understanding tasks by significant margins, demonstrating the effectiveness of the Open OmniCoach framework. In particular, Open OmniAgent-30B improves average accuracy over Qwen3-Omni by 6.3 percentage points across nine benchmarks, with gains of 12.2 and 13.3 points on LVOmniBench and LVBench, respectively.
  </details>

- **[Generalizable Feed-Forward 3D Hand-Object Reconstruction with Coarse-to-Fine Contact Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31812)**  `arXiv:2611.31812`  `cs.CV`  
  _Seonghwan Park, Yong-Hoon Kwon, Min-Gyu Park_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing the 3D poses of hands and objects from a single image, together with the contact between them, is fundamental to understanding manipulation. To be practical, the reconstruction must generalize to unseen objects, and the contact must be localized precisely. Yet joint feed-forward estimators often rely on predefined object sets, while contact is typically predicted from image features alone, without the explicit geometry needed to localize it. To address both limitations, we present HORCRUX, a unified feed-forward transformer with coarse-to-fine contact estimation. For unseen objects, a frozen foundation model encodes the object from a given or image-reconstructed mesh rather than from object-specific knowledge. A shared transformer then reasons jointly over the object and one or two hands to keep them aligned. For precise contact, coarse per-joint predictions from latent features are resolved over finer hand regions using the reconstructed geometry. Across seven benchmarks, HORCRUX achieves state-of-the-art object and hand pose accuracy among joint estimators and outperforms a dedicated contact estimator in fine-grained contact. These results suggest that contact is best estimated jointly with reconstruction, whose geometry directly localizes it.
  </details>

- **[SceneMemBench: A Benchmark for Scene Memory in Interactive Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31803)**  `arXiv:2611.31803`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuheng Si, Linye Lyu, Jiawei Zhou, Lingyi Du, Nanfei Ye, Aobo Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive video world models generate navigable worlds frame by frame, but a persistent world requires previously observed scenes to remain recoverable as users move away and return. Existing memory evaluations, however, often focus on seen-view revisits and full rollouts, leaving cross-view scene recovery underexplored while entangling memory with trajectory-following errors and accumulated generation degradation. We introduce SceneMemBench, a benchmark for scene memory in interactive video world models. It evaluates two complementary dimensions: seen-view recall versus novel-view recovery, and controlled diagnosis versus end-to-end interaction. We introduce Continued Rollout from a shared ground-truth observation history, alongside Full Rollout over complete model-generated interactions. SceneMemBench contains 600 controlled trajectories across 33 Unreal Engine scenes and 60 Full Rollout cases, covering eight representative open- and closed-source models. We evaluate scene recovery along subject consistency, appearance fidelity, and geometric consistency. Results show that novel-view recovery is generally more challenging than seen-view recall, while model profiles under controlled histories can differ markedly from those observed in full interaction. Together, these findings reveal complementary failure modes obscured by seen-view revisits or end-to-end rollouts alone.
  </details>

- **[Dreamcatcher: Remembering What Surprises a World Model in Video Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31765)**  `arXiv:2611.31765`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jinxi He, Ce Zhang, Wenhe Liu, Jianshu Zhang, Katia P. Sycara, Yaqi Xie_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As live video streams grow longer, repeatedly encoding and processing their full histories becomes increasingly costly. Answering questions as they arrive therefore calls for visual memory built in one pass before future questions are known. Existing methods mainly keep tokens that differ from earlier ones or match surrogate queries fixed in advance. Without modeling world dynamics, these criteria struggle to tell subtle surprising details from expected change. We introduce Dreamcatcher, a training-free framework and, to our knowledge, the first to use predictive signals from an independently pretrained, frozen video world model for online memory formation in streaming video question answering. Conditioned on recent history, the world model predicts incoming features using learned world priors. Its normalized prediction errors yield temporal and spatial surprisal signals that guide online segmentation and the retention of a frozen Video-LLM's native visual tokens, balancing surprising details with broad visual coverage under budgets that decay with segment age. At query time, an evidence gate admits a segment from a similarity-based shortlist only when the Video-LLM judges that it adds evidence for the question beyond the current view. We evaluate Dreamcatcher on three streaming and three offline long-video benchmarks. On OVO-Bench, Dreamcatcher achieves the highest overall accuracy among the compared training-free methods for each of four frozen Video-LLMs, reaching 78.9% with Qwen3.8-27B. With the three Qwen Video-LLMs, it also reduces average input tokens for answer generation by more than 80% relative to their base models at 1 fps.
  </details>

- **[Xtream: An Explicit Metric State for Large-Scale Streaming Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31757)**  `arXiv:2611.31757`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ernesto Lozano, Alberto Jaenal, Javier Civera_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D foundation models (3DFMs) enable dense reconstruction in a feed-forward pass, yet their effective span is limited by cross-view attention. Local consistency has been expanded through geometric alignment of video chunks and recursive updates of an implicit memory, and large-scale scenes with pose graphs. However, both strategies are prone to drift, largely due to the lack of metric-scale observability from monocular geometry. We present Xtream,a hybrid streaming pipeline that maintains an explicit metric state through a factor graph over keyframes, while using a 3DFM as a geometric front end. Xtream incorporates metric information in two ways: through metric scale predictions from 3DFMs; or through inertial measurements if available. Across the large-scale benchmarks of VBR, Oxford Spires and KITTI, Xtream achieves state-of-the-art performance in 3DFM-based online reconstruction, reducing average absolute trajectory errors compared with published baselines and concurrent work.this https URL
  </details>

- **[NOBODY Was There: Bridging the Human-VLM Gap in Unwarranted Person Attribution Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31714)**  `arXiv:2611.31714`  `cs.CV`  
  _Parsa Madinei, Ziqi Wen, Miguel Eckstein_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Describing an image requires not only recognizing what is visible but deciding what should be inferred. We investigate a systematic gap between humans and VLMs in their tendency to attribute visible actions or events to people who are not present in an image. We term this behavior unwarranted person attribution bias and find that, across 25 SOTA VLMs, captions of person-absent scenes mention a person four times as often as human-written captions of the same images. Through a preference study, we further show that both humans and VLMs prefer captions that avoid such attributions. Motivated by this, we introduce NOBODY, a preference dataset designed to bridge the gap between humans and VLMs in unwarranted person attribution. Through direct preference optimization (DPO), we align open VLMs on NOBODY and show that their agreement with human captions on person attribution reaches or exceeds inter-human agreement, whereas nine existing public preference datasets leave this behavior largely unchanged. Furthermore, we show that human judges preferred the aligned VLM captions significantly more than those of the baseline model. Alongside NOBODY, we release NOBODY-Bench, which evaluates VLMs for unwarranted person attribution bias and their alignment against human captions and preference. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[E-Waste-Bench: A Large-Scale Benchmark for Visual Triage of Data-Center Hardware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31703)**  `arXiv:2611.31703`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nader Lobandi, Mohammad Mehdi Hosseini, Lombe Chileshe, Mohammad Mahoor_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Retired data-center hardware can often be refurbished or resold, but triage demands more than object categories. A model must tell visually similar parts apart, judge condition under damage and occlusion, and read the printed part number. We audit nine public e-waste collections. They cover mostly consumer devices (only 10% of images are labeled as a data-center part), label neither condition nor printed text, and contain 42% exact or augmented duplicates. We introduce E-Waste-Bench, 328K photographs drawn from 762K deduplicated images and labeled for class (24 classes), granularity, condition and printed text. Images come from those collections and the web, where a recipe-based crawler works beside an agent built on an open-weight vision-language model. The agent vets new sources and reads sites the crawler cannot parse, and both are steered toward under-represented classes. Training labels are model-made, splits follow near-duplicate groups, and human annotators label a test set of nearly 10,000 images. We benchmark classification, condition, detection, text perception and cross-dataset transfer. On this test set, a ConvNeXt-T trained on E-Waste-Bench reaches 64.3% top-1, against 31.5% with the public collections alone. Near-duplicate leakage inflates top-1 by 3.0 points under model-made test labels but by less than 0.1 under human labels, so model-labeled benchmarks need group splits. Accuracy falls to 42.1% on marketplace and auction photographs, the closest proxy for hardware leaving service. Data and code will be released.
  </details>

- **[Lit Up but Not Needed: Scoped Lesions Show Where Multimodal Mixture-of-Experts Depend on Their Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31576)**  `arXiv:2611.31576`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhixuan Ge, Anqi Li, Kexin Geng, Wei Qiu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cognitive neuroscience learned that an activation map is not a map of necessity. Regions that light up during a task are not always needed for it, and only lesions reveal which ones are. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) vision-language models come with an activation map for free, since the router records which experts respond to image and text tokens, and recent work reads modality-specialized experts off this map. We bring the lesion. In Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B, Kimi-VL-A3B and Gemma-4-26B-A4B, 3 to 12% of the experts are vision-exclusive: image tokens route to them at up to five times the uniform rate and text tokens almost never. In Qwen3-VL they receive a quarter of all image-token routing, and lesioning all of them costs less than one percentage point on average over eight perception benchmarks and free-form OCR, no more than lesioning a random set of the same size. In Kimi-VL and Gemma-4 the lesion also costs no more than a random set. Scoped lesions, which remove an expert set only at image positions or only at text positions, then show where the models do depend on their experts. In Qwen3-VL, silencing every expert at every image position costs 3 points, against 45 at text positions, where the dependence sits in dual-use experts that text-only tasks need as well. Across models the image-side dependence is 2 to 3 points in Qwen3-VL and Kimi-VL and 26 in Gemma-4. Reading text in images is the one ability that needs image-position experts in every model.
