The exclusive experts receive informative patches, produce outputs of ordinary size, and stay inert under sensory deprivation: text-only training recruits them only when load balancing forces it. In Kimi-VL the balancing bias itself manufactures most of them. Load balancing draws the modality map in the part of the computation the models depend on least. Expert selection, pruning and skipping should follow the lesion, not the map.
  </details>

- **[InitFree-SfM: When Initialization-Free Bundle Adjustment meets Learned Structure-from-Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31516)**  `arXiv:2611.31516`  `cs.CV`  
  _Simon Weber, Mateo de Mayo, Je Hyeong Hong, Carl Olsson, Daniel Cremers, Ronald Clark_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Initialization-free bundle adjustment (InitFree BA) aims to jointly recover camera poses and scene structure without requiring an initial global estimate. It is a very challenging problem, especially compared to traditional BA where a good estimation is assumed. While recent methods optimize surrogate Object Space Error (OSE) objectives from random initialization, a low objective value does not necessarily yield an accurate reconstruction, revealing a gap between optimization and reconstruction. We show that existing InitFree BA pipelines struggle to produce accurate 3D reconstructions on modern SfM benchmarks, and demonstrate that incorporating learned metric depth substantially improves their practical feasibility. We introduce InitFree-SfM, a complete initialization-free SfM pipeline combining learned correspondences, metric depth, depth-consistent track construction, and joint metric refinement. Within this framework, we propose a simple depth-augmented OSE (dOSE), which complements the directional constraints of OSE by constraining landmark positions along their viewing rays. Our approach substantially outperforms existing InitFree BA methods and achieves competitive accuracy with established SfM pipelines, including on challenging low-overlap image collections. Importantly, our analysis challenges the prevailing emphasis on surrogate objectives in previous InitFree BA research, revealing that geometric constraints play a central role in accurate reconstruction, while OSE-based optimization provides further improvements.
  </details>

- **[HyMTS: A Multiscale Tensor Factorization-Based Network for Hyperspectral Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31506)**  `arXiv:2611.31506`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lei Xu, Fahad Sohrab, Moncef Gabbouj, Mehmet Yamac_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Hyperspectral image (HSI) restoration has been explored using deep learning techniques recently. Because hyperspectral images have strong spectral and spatial redundancy, the underlying clean signal is usually represented in a low-dimensional spectral subspace and locally low-rank spatial structures. Existing methods primarily explore dense attention or feature mixing across spectral bands. In this work, we propose HyMTS, a hyperspectral restoration network with a backbone built on the Multi-scale Tensorial Summation (MTS) factorization operator. HyMTS provides a natural architectural mechanism for exploiting a low-rank prior, embedded directly in the network operator rather than imposed via an auxiliary loss or a constraint on the reconstructed output. Specifically, HyMTS stacks a unified block that couples factorized layers with input-adaptive layers, capturing both local and global dependencies across the spectral and spatial dimensions. Extensive experiments without auxiliary data on standard benchmarks for Gaussian and real-noise denoising and single-image super-resolution show that HyMTS efficiently achieves superior or comparable performance to various state-of-the-art approaches.
  </details>

- **[GDD: A Remote Sensing Image Deepfake Detection and Manipulation Localization Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31487)**  `arXiv:2611.31487`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jacob Arndt, Philipe Dias, Debvrat Varshney, Sudarshan Srinivasan, Dalton Lunga_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Verifying image authenticity is a critical need for a variety of applications and decision-making in the remote sensing (RS) domain, yet it is becoming more challenging as advancements in generative AI enable producing increasingly realistic synthetic overhead data. However, existing RS deepfake benchmarks prioritize sheer volume at the expense of visual realism and diversity, while also lacking rigorous out-of-distribution (OOD) testing protocols to evaluate real-world robustness. To address this, we introduce the Geospatial Deepfakes Dataset (GDD), a benchmark for RS deepfake detection and manipulation localization. Rather than relying on uncurated, redundant synthetic generation, GDD comprises images across diverse modern generative models and real image sources, alongside manipulations from diffusion inpainting, splicing, and copy-move operations. Crucially, GDD establishes an OOD evaluation protocol for deepfake detection comprising unseen real image sources and generative models. We benchmark five deepfake detectors and two localization models across intra- and cross-dataset regimes and reveal that detectors trained on high-volume, uncurated benchmarks overfit to dataset-specific artifacts, whereas training on GDD yields superior cross-dataset generalization despite its compact scale. This benchmarking also exposes critical failure modes in current state-of-the-art methods. Combined, GDD and its evaluation protocol establish a high-quality standard for developing and stress-testing detection and localization models in this safety-critical RS domain. The dataset is publicly available.
  </details>

- **[Which Way Is Up? Rotated Photos Silently Hurt Multi-View 3D Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31478)**  `arXiv:2611.31478`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yukun Shi, Minglun Gong_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-view 3D foundation models estimate camera poses and 3D points from a set of photos in one forward pass. Many cameras store a portrait shot sideways, with an orientation tag telling viewers how to turn it, and this tag can be ignored, lost or wrong. We measure how rotated photos affect five such models on three datasets. A few rotated photos lower camera pose accuracy for every model, and the sideways photos already in a standard benchmark do the same. Even the most robust model still loses accuracy. The failure is also silent: the models' confidence does not reliably flag it, and most official image loaders ignore the tag. The fixes we test each correct only part of the problem. These findings call for orientation checks in reconstruction pipelines and benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[PromptSMC: Enabling Particle Exploration in Flow Matching via LLM-Based Prompt Rejuvenation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31471)**  `arXiv:2611.31471`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wenyi Lian, Zheng Zhao, Thomas B. Schön_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper is concerned with prompt-image alignment at inference time through exploration of generative trajectories and dynamical prompt rejuvenation. We leverage sequential Monte Carlo (SMC), to offer a gradient-free and principled framework for this purpose through reward-guided trajectory selection. More specifically, we introduce LLM-based prompt rejuvenation, allowing for efficient particle exploration in SMC samplers along the diffusion time in flow matching models. The proposed method, PromptSMC, retains the selected latent states and uses a pretrained LLM to dynamically revise the prompts of duplicated particles under semantic-preservation constraints. These revised prompts induce alternative conditional velocity fields, allowing particles to explore different continuations from a shared intermediate state. The method further uses a reward model to evaluate each particle's alignment with the original prompt, and the resulting scores guide particles through resampling. PromptSMC thus integrates prompt-based exploration with reward-guided sampling, achieving faithful prompt-image alignment without additional noise injection, model training, or reward gradients. Our experiments and ablation studies across multiple benchmarks and backbones demonstrate the effectiveness of the method for text-to-image flow matching. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Optimising VLM Harnesses to Localise Errors in Generated Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30673)**  `arXiv:2611.30673`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ankit Yadav, Nikhil Cherian Kurian, Arpit Garg, Lingqiao Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Identifying subtle errors in generated videos is an important step toward automated video generation, where errors must be detected to assess and refine generated clips. Recent work shows that pretrained vision-language models (VLMs) can localise such errors, but their effectiveness depends strongly on the workflow used: choices about which frames to inspect, which tools and model calls to make, and how to combine predictions can lead to substantially different results. We study VLM harnesses for video error localisation. Instead of relying on manually designed harnesses, we propose Harness Search and Merge (HSM), an algorithm that automatically constructs them by searching over executable evaluation programs. An LLM orchestrator refines candidate harnesses independently on random training subsets, records findings in a memory, and constructively merges complementary candidates. We evaluate HSM on Spotlight, a recently proposed benchmark for localising errors in generated videos. HSM achieves 0.234 $\pm$ 0.012 F1, exceeding the strongest benchmark strategy (0.173 $\pm$ 0.016), and retains its lead on an unseen video generator (0.213 $\pm$ 0.008 versus 0.140 $\pm$ 0.003). On IPV-Bench, it achieves strong localisation performance with lower false-alarm rates than existing optimisation baselines. Together, these results show that searching over complete VLM harnesses can outperform existing evaluation strategies, transfer to an unseen video generator, and reduce false alarms on real videos.
  </details>

- **[SVUBench: A Comprehensive Benchmark for Proactive Streaming Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30541)**  `arXiv:2611.30541`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuxuan Liang, Bohan Zhang, Haiyang Yu, Bocheng Hu, Ke Niu, Xu Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Proactive streaming video understanding requires models to interpret unfolding events, respond timely, and retrieve relevant historical evidence. To systematically evaluate these capabilities, we introduce SVUBench, a comprehensive proactive streaming video benchmark comprising 12,131 questions and 62,881 answer points across ten proactive tasks spanning Perception, Understanding, and Reasoning. Constructed through model-assisted annotation and human verification, SVUBench introduces two complementary interaction modes. Basic evaluates sustained proactive responses from video onset, while Trajectory requires historical recovery and continuous response updates following task activation during streaming. A Duration-Scaled Challenge further evaluates these capabilities across four video duration tiers. We develop the Proactive Response Score (PRS) with task-adaptive answer windows to jointly assess answer correctness, response timeliness, historical consistency, and content quality. Extensive experiments and analysis reveal the strengths and limitations of existing models, highlighting directions for advancing proactive streaming video understanding.
  </details>

- **[PROPS: Can Visual Generators Keep the Current Look of a Prop?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29665)**  `arXiv:2611.29665`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiangzhao Hao, Dianyi Wang, Keyan Zhou, Yiqian Zhang, Zefeng Zhang, Bailin Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Many generation tasks draw the next image from a visual history, such as storyboards, illustrated stories and multi-turn editing. A new request often states what happens next without restating how earlier objects look. Instead of recovering an object's current look from the history, generators often redraw it from the request alone or bring back an outdated look, and the error can carry into later steps. We call this failure prop drift. Existing evaluations rarely isolate it, because similarity to the history rewards copying and a judge that sees a single history cannot tell a look recovered from that history from one the generator happens to prefer. We introduce PROPS, a benchmark of 2,257 pairs of twin histories that end on the same request but show one prop in two different looks. A pair passes only if the generator draws the matching look on each side and carries out the request, so a look preferred by the generator or the judge passes at most one side. All 34 open and closed systems we test show prop drift, and even systems that carry out the requested action on most sides often lose the current look of the prop. Swapping the planners and editors of agents further shows that both history understanding and image generation limit agent performance. We also build PROPS-Train, 80,250 training chains in four tracks whose targets recover an earlier state or carry scripted changes forward, and fine-tuning on it clearly improves all four tested models. Both datasets will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[PSC-Gen: Physical Sequence Conditioning for Video Generation via Agentic Visual Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28756)**  `arXiv:2611.28756`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wonsik Shin, Jinju Jang, Junhoo Lee, Yeonjin Chang, Jimyeong Kim, Nojun Kwak_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in video diffusion models have enabled the synthesis of high-fidelity, visually compelling videos. However, despite being trained on internet-scale video data, understanding underlying physical laws and generating physically plausible dynamics remain challenging. To mitigate this, conditioning video generators on explicit physical reasoning has emerged as a promising direction. Nevertheless, existing conditioning approaches often fail to incorporate detailed physical guidance into generation, resulting in physically implausible motion or visual artifacts. We propose PSC-Gen, a training-free framework that employs an agentic approach to synthesize visual conditions and inject their core dynamics via low-frequency filtering. By iteratively planning physical states and verifying generated keyframes, our Physics Agent constructs visually and physically consistent keyframe sequences. We then interpolate these keyframes into a continuous physical sequence and inject only its low-frequency components, steering the video model to preserve physical dynamics while synthesizing fine visual details. Qualitative and quantitative evaluations across physics-informed and robotic manipulation benchmarks demonstrate that PSC-Gen faithfully follows instructions and generates physically plausible videos.
  </details>

- **[Domain-Specific Self-Supervised Learning for Cuneiform Sign Representation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28712)**  `arXiv:2611.28712`  `cs.CV`  
  _Asim Niaz, Morris Alper, Shai Gordin, Enrique Jiménez_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recognizing cuneiform signs is challenging: sign shapes vary across historical periods, scribal practices, and preservation conditions, while expert annotations are scarce. We study whether domain-specific self-supervised pretraining yields better cuneiform sign representations than generic natural-image pretraining. We pretrain a masked autoencoder, CuneiMAE, on over 900K unlabeled sign crops and compare it with ImageNet-pretrained MAE, CLIP, and DINOv2 on a 218-class sign recognition benchmark. CuneiMAE learns substantially stronger frozen representations (43.7% linear-probe accuracy vs. 25.2% for ImageNet-MAE), with gains over ImageNet-MAE on cross-period transfer. Under fine-tuning, CuneiMAE outperforms ImageNet-pretrained baselines on our internal benchmark, with the largest gains when labels are scarce (+20.4 pp over ImageNet-MAE with 1% of labels). On an external benchmark, CuneiMAE reaches 69.9% top-1 accuracy after fine-tuning, about 8 pp above the ResNet-18 baseline from prior work. Explicit structural priors, such as edge-guided masking and part bottlenecks, do not improve over standard masked reconstruction. Overall, domain-specific self-supervision shows strong performance on cuneiform recognition tasks, particularly when labels are limited.
  </details>

- **[CryoGaussian: Heterogeneous Cryo-EM Reconstruction Made Explicit with Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.26128)**  `arXiv:2611.26128`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ziwen Wang, Jiajun Fan, Chaoran Cheng, Ruihan Guo, Yanru Qu, Juexiao Zhou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Single-particle cryo-electron microscopy (cryo-EM) determines macromolecular structures from noisy particle images. However, rare, transient compositional states and subtle, continuous conformational variation are difficult to resolve. We present CryoGaussian, a Gaussian splatting method for heterogeneous cryo-EM reconstruction that fits an explicit Gaussian density representation directly to the particle images through a differentiable rasterizer rendering orthographic line integrals modulated by the contrast transfer function. The Gaussians are initialized randomly, with their number derived from the estimated spatial support of the molecular density and their minimum size bounded by the Nyquist frequency of the particle images. To capture the compositional and conformational variation across particle images, we learn particle-image and per-Gaussian latents that jointly modulate the base position, amplitude, and scale of each Gaussian. Each structural state is then reconstructed by fitting the Gaussians conditioned on its inferred latent. On CryoBench, CryoGaussian achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction accuracy for both compositional and conformational heterogeneity. On experimental data, it resolves a rare compositional state of the 50S ribosomal subunit (class C4, 1.3% of particles) with rRNA helix 68 docked in the absence of the central protuberance, and continuous conformational motion of the SF3b and helicase subcomplexes of the pre-catalytic spliceosome. We further optimize the rasterizer for additive cryo-EM projection, reducing training time by 12.8-28.0$\times$. Reconstructed density maps are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[RS-JEPA: Remote Sensing Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture with Scan-Path Views for Cross-Sensor Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14496)**  `arXiv:2611.14496`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tanishq Rachamalla, Srishti Kaushik, Muhammad Attique Khan, Swalpa Kumar Roy_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cross-sensor remote sensing image retrieval aims to find scenes with the same content across heterogeneous sensors, such as SAR and optical or panchromatic (PAN) and multispectral (MS) imagery, without manual labels. Existing self-supervised methods either reconstruct sensor-specific pixel detail, align each image only with its co-registered twin, or rely on large transformers with 118-173M parameters (and up to 398M). We introduce RS-JEPA, a compact joint-embedding predictive framework built on a Vision Mamba encoder shared by both sensors. Since a state-space encoder reads an image as an ordered sequence of patches, we propose scan-path views, which treat two reading orders of the same image as two views with identical pixels and predict the hidden patches in one order from the visible patches in thethis http URLkeep this prediction challenging, we propose Adversarial View Coupling (AVC), which learns the hardest split of the visible patches, and a neighbourhood consensus loss that aligns the scene similarities of both sensors. Extensive experiments on SAR-optical (BEN-14K, CBRSIR-VS) and PAN-MS (DSRSID) benchmarks demonstrate that RS-JEPA achieves the highest F1@5 among twelve re-implemented methods (72.92, 80.35 and 94.43) with a retrieval network of only 3.88M parameters (14.57M in training, 8-16times fewer than prior JEPA retrievers). Like prior methods, RS-JEPA trains on co-registered pairs. Our results highlight reading order as a source of self-supervised views. code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Rethinking Geometric Depth in Monocular 3D Object Detection: A Projection-Consistent Reformulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11607)**  `arXiv:2611.11607`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhihao Zhang, Abhinav Kumar, Huaizhi Qu, Tianlong Chen, Xiaoming Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Monocular 3D detection (Mono3D) hinges on recovering depth from a single image, for which the geometric formulation $z \approx f \cdot H / h_{2D}$ is widely adopted due to its simplicity and direct grounding in projective geometry. We show that this formulation harbors a systematic, projection-induced bias: substituting the 2D bounding-box height $h_{2D}$ recovers the depth of an object's visible front surface rather than its center, causing size-dependent underestimation that persists even under ground-truth inputs. We address this front-surface bias with MonoPCA, a unified framework built on a projection-consistent reformulation. At its core, PCDepth retains the measurable $h_{2D}$ and closes the gap to center depth through an orientation- and size-aware correction derived from perspective geometry, eliminating the bias without introducing ill-posed regression targets. Because PCDepth couples depth to predicted object size and orientation, attribute perception becomes essential. We therefore introduce GeoAlign, a teacher-student framework that transfers geometric representations from Vision Foundation Models into a lightweight student, strengthening attribute perception at no inference cost. Across four challenging benchmarks, MonoPCA achieves SoTA performance, including a +1.44% AP$_{3D}$ gain on the KITTI leaderboard and +2.80% on Omni3D$_{OUT}$. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Parse Before You Execute: Efficient Tool-Integrated Spatial Reasoning Agents with Executable Primitives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.41389)**  `arXiv:2610.41389`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiwei Liu, Yulong Li, Xinling Zhuang, Xuhui Li, Yichen Li, Haolin Yang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatial reasoning in Vision-Language Models (VLMs) has increasingly benefited from tool-integrated pipelines that ground computation in explicit geometry. However, existing systems often compile queries into relatively open-ended artifacts, such as code, task constraints, tool plans, or gathered evidence, leaving what must be resolved before execution under-specified. This can lead to unnecessary tool acquisition and iterative refinement, while still producing plausible reasoning traces from incomplete spatial commitments. We identify this failure mode as under-committed query compilation. To address it, we propose PEPS, a training-free agent Parsing Executable Primitives for Spatial reasoning under a typed FESM schema over Frame, Entity, State, and Metric. PEPS follows a parse-before-execute principle: the Parser commits each query to a compact set of executable requirements; the Executor selectively grounds the required primitives through fixed geometric APIs and deterministic computation; and the Verifier checks whether these requirements are fulfilled, enabling early termination or targeted repair. Experiments on three spatial reasoning benchmarks and four VLM backbones show that PEPS improves reasoning accuracy while substantially reducing refinement rounds, API cost, and end-to-end latency over strong tool-integrated baselines. These results demonstrate that knowing what a query requires before execution makes tool-integrated spatial reasoning not only more reliable, but also more efficient.
  </details>

- **[OVM-Occ: Open-Vocabulary Monocular Occupancy Prediction via Geometry-Aware Vision-Language Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35312)**  `arXiv:2610.35312`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuyang Yu, Haibo Qin, Shaohan Hu, Wei Shan, Xiaoqing Guan, You Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Monocular occupancy prediction in indoor scenes aims to recover a scene's 3D geometry and semantics from a single RGB image. Existing methods are restricted to a closed set of predefined categories, limiting their ability to generalize to unseen categories. This limitation is particularly critical in indoor scenes, where objects exhibit highly diverse and fine-grained semantic categories. We study open-vocabulary monocular occupancy prediction for indoor scenes and present OVM-Occ, a Gaussian-based occupancy framework with an instance-aware vision-language teacher. Specifically, we adopt Language-Embedded Gaussians as the intermediate scene representation, propose a geometry-aware deformable attention module to refine Gaussian semantic features, and construct instance-level vision-language embeddings to provide reliable semantic supervision. We further introduce geometry-aware decoupled semantic distillation to mitigate the interference between geometry learning and semantic learning. To evaluate open-vocabulary occupancy prediction performance, we introduce OpenOcc-ScanNet and OpenOcc-Replica, two extended benchmarks with more diverse and fine-grained semantic categories. Experiments show that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance in open-vocabulary monocular occupancy prediction for indoor scenes, outperforming the previous state-of-the-art approach by 12.2 mIoU and 0.6 IoU on the Occ-ScanNet dataset, with consistent gains under new open-vocabulary evaluation protocols.
  </details>

- **[ShadowCast: On-the-Fly Context-Aware Shadow Augmentation for Remote Sensing Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23440)**  `arXiv:2610.23440`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dongho Yoon, Yechan Kim, Jongmin Joo, JongHyun Park, Moongu Jeon_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Shadows cast by elevated objects alter the spectral appearance and obscure the boundaries of objects in remote sensing images, thereby degrading semantic segmentation performance. Most existing approaches handle shadows as degradations to be detected and removed, requiring additional costs from dedicated networks and shadow-specific annotations. We propose ShadowCast, which takes the opposite approach: it synthesizes shadows and injects them during training for remote sensing segmentation. It extracts masks of elevated objects, such as buildings and trees, directly from the segmentation labels and estimates an image-specific cast-shadow direction cue by probing the brightness around them. It then projects synthetic shadows along the inferred cast-shadow direction, requiring no learned network and no acquisition metadata. Experiments on three widely used remote sensing benchmarks, Vaihingen, Potsdam, and LoveDA, show that ShadowCast consistently outperforms the baseline across diverse state-of-the-art segmentation architectures. Moreover, it brings additional performance gains when combined with standard augmentation and regularization techniques, indicating that stochastic shadow injection serves as a simple and complementary augmentation strategy for remote sensing segmentation.
  </details>

- **[Image Diffusion Models Are Good 3D Human Motion Generators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14254)**  `arXiv:2610.14254`  `cs.CV`  
  _Siyuan Yang, Chong Wang, Hao Cheng, Jun Liu, Bihan Wen, Alex Kot, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-motion generation typically relies on dataset-specific skeletal representations, with models trained separately for benchmarks with different joint layouts. We investigate whether image diffusion architectures can model 3D human motion through a common image-compatible representation that accommodates heterogeneous skeletal layouts while preserving motion-specific kinematic structure. To this end, we organize root-centered joint trajectories into topology-informed joint--time images and model global root dynamics through a complementary latent stream. A motion autoencoder conditions local-motion decoding on the root latent, while a text-conditioned Scalable Interpolant Transformer (SiT) jointly generates both latent streams, with supervision on decoded 3D joint positions further constraining generation in motion space. In addition to dataset-specific models, we train a single motion autoencoder on HumanML3D and KIT-ML, followed by a single text-conditioned SiT shared across both datasets, preserving their respective skeletal definitions without skeleton retargeting to a common joint layout. Experiments on HumanML3D and KIT-ML show that our dataset-specific models achieve state-of-the-art performance, while shared training yields further gains on both benchmarks, demonstrating the benefits of shared modeling across heterogeneous skeletal layouts.
  </details>

- **[Seek-and-View Reasoning for Multi-View Spatial Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11810)**  `arXiv:2610.11810`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qixiang Chen, Cheng Zhang, Fucai Ke, Chi-Wing Fu, Jianfei Cai, Jingwen Ye_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing multi-view spatial reasoning remains constrained by sparse and fixed viewpoints. Consequently, vision-language models (VLMs) must infer spatial relations solely from these fixed views, leading to fragile cross-view alignment and a geometry-to-language bottleneck. To overcome these issues, we formulate a novel Seek-and-View reasoning paradigm that seeks a viewpoint to make the queried spatial relation directly observable and interprets the sought view to support the spatial reasoning. Building on this paradigm, we propose Vantage, a training-free and model-agnostic framework that structures VLM reasoning for viewpoint planning and evidence-augmented spatial understanding, with a 3D foundation model supporting view synthesis. Specifically, we develop a viewpoint-grounded reasoning stage that translates spatial queries into targeted viewpoint plans. A subsequent geometry-grounded evidence augmentation stage synthesizes new views and incorporates visual evidence alongside preceding reasoning contexts for final answering. Together, these stages reduce reliance on language-based cross-view alignment. Extensive experiments on six VLMs across six benchmarks show consistent improvements in overall accuracy, with an average absolute gain of 8.2% without fine-tuning.
  </details>

- **[GS-Pool: Object-Level Change Detection in 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06688)**  `arXiv:2610.06688`  `cs.CV`  
  _Boaz Keren-Gil, James Gain, Patrick Marais_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Many physical spaces require photographic monitoring of changes to their structure over time. When each visit is reconstructed with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS), a direct comparison of the two reconstructions cannot answer this because training is stochastic and two reconstructions of an unchanged space never coincide. To address this, we propose GS-Pool, which takes two independently reconstructed Gaussian scans of the same space and returns the changed objects in each, together with their change masks. Our method begins by extracting SAM2 masks from the photographs of each visit. These are then lifted onto the Gaussians that render them and merged into an object pool, a collection of 3D objects, so every decision is taken once per object in 3D. We introduce a novel photographic carrier approach, which co-opts the existing 3DGS mechanism. It does so by applying the photographs of the other visit and back-propagating them to the Gaussians that rendered each pixel. We combine this with GS-Diff's geometry and colour terms and our distilled DINOv3 features. This evidence is compared with objects identified as unchanged in both visits to set a change threshold for each scene. On the PASLCD benchmark, GS-Pool achieves mIoU/F1 scores of 0.75/0.85 against 0.64/0.76 for GS-Diff, the strongest offline method for detecting change in 3DGS. Finally, we return each changed object as a set of Gaussians, which can be inspected in 3D.
  </details>

- **[EgoPathBench: Evaluating Zero-Shot Egocentric Waypoint Decision-Making in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16610)**  `arXiv:2609.16610`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yang Zhao, Zhuo Chen, Xubo Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Widely used spatial-QA benchmarks increasingly motivate embodied navigation, yet evaluate foundation vision-language models (VLMs) through isolated queries about space rather than feasible action sequences in space. However, zero-shot waypoint navigation requires turning spatial understanding into a feasible action sequence: from a single first-person view, a model must jointly ground the target, respect agent-specific clearance, maintain edge-by-edge legality, and stop at the goal. To evaluate this capability, we introduce EgoPathBench, a dataset and five-task benchmark for first-person waypoint decision-making. Each example pairs an egocentric RGB image, a natural-language goal, and numbered visible waypoints, and scores predicted candidate sets or ordered routes against registered 3D scene geometry under task-specific Point or Embodied feasibility graphs. EgoPathBench comprises 31,852 training, 1,345 validation, and 1,111 benchmark questions, with at least one verified reference route for every route question. Across ten foundation VLMs, the highest EgoPath Score is only 35.6. While models often make locally plausible choices and the top model reaches 41.7% success on Point Path, it drops to 10.0% on both Embodied Path and Intent Path, with the remaining nine models below 3% on Embodied Path (and at most 4.0% on Intent Path) -- exposing a sharp gap between local plausibility and complete embodied routes. Beyond evaluation, we construct the EgoPath-SFT training set. Fine-tuning Qwen3.5-4B on EgoPath-SFT raises its EgoPath Score from 3.9 to 38.9, improves closed-loop Isaac Sim execution by 20 percentage points, and transfers consistently to three external spatial benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[ArmorOCR: Grounded Adversarial Visual Perception via Observation-Transferred Self-Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20122)**  `arXiv:2608.20122`  `cs.CV`  
  _Linhan Cao, Siyuan Li, Jun Lan, Liangbo He, Guannan Li, Xiaolei Huang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large multimodal models (LMMs) have demonstrated strong OCR recognition capabilities, yet remain vulnerable to adversarial visual text that is readable to humans but challenging for models to localize and recognize. Existing OCR benchmarks mainly focus on natural or document-style text, while adversarial OCR evaluations remain limited in scale, task coverage, or region-aware evaluation. In this paper, we formulate adversarial OCR as a \textbf{grounded OCR perception} task and introduce \textbf{AdvSpot}, the first benchmark for grounded adversarial OCR evaluation. AdvSpot comprises 383 images with region-level annotations, spanning 5 primary categories and 13 fine-grained adversarial OCR types. To address this challenge, we propose \textbf{ArmorOCR}, a two-stage training framework for robust adversarial OCR perception. ArmorOCR first acquires missing adversarial OCR perception from privileged transformed observations through On-Policy Self-Distillation (OPSD), and then refines grounded OCR perception through Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) with task-conditioned rewards for localization, recognition, full spotting, and visual question answering (VQA). Experiments on our AdvSpot, other adversarial OCR benchmarks, and general OCR benchmarks demonstrate that ArmorOCR consistently improves adversarial OCR perception while preserving competitive general OCR capability. The benchmark and code are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[AdaGEM: Granularity Adaptation and Geospatial Evidence Modulation for Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.15075)**  `arXiv:2608.15075`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kexin Ma, Jing Xiao, Bowen Xing, Liang Liao, Chia-Wen Lin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing imagery provides rich spatial details and extensive geographic coverage for Earth observation understanding. However, such imagery generates quadratically growing visual token sequences, where sparse task-critical evidence is easily overwhelmed by redundant and task-irrelevant tokens, leading to excessive computation and ineffective evidence preservation. Existing methods seek to balance computational efficiency and task effectiveness through selective multi-scale inspection or direct token pruning, yet often overlook task-dependent granularity requirements and struggle to distinguish sparse critical evidence from overwhelming visual redundancy. To address these challenges, we propose AdaGEM, a unified framework that jointly adapts visual granularity to task demands and modulates token selection to preserve essential geospatial evidence. Specifically, a Granularity Router predicts detail requirements and evidence scope from the query, then selects input resolution and token budget via a rule-based mapping. At the adapted granularity, a Geospatial Evidence Modulator selects complementary visual tokens to preserve critical evidence and spatial context while suppressing redundancy in homogeneous regions. Extensive experiments across diverse remote sensing benchmarks and multiple MLLM backbones demonstrate that AdaGEM consistently improves task performance over strong baselines and existing UHR methods while substantially reducing inference costs.
  </details>

- **[DistMoE: Private-data Rehearsal-free Routing in Mixture-of-Experts for Distributed Instruction Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09907)**  `arXiv:2608.09907`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mainak Singha, Niccolò Biondi, Elisa Ricci, Subhankar Roy_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have shown strong multimodal instruction-following ability, but adapting them to diverse visual-language domains typically assumes centralized data access and costly joint training. This is restrictive when data is distributed across private, domain-specific, or permission-limited clients. To this end, we propose DistMoE, a mixture-of-experts (MoE) approach for distributed visual instruction tuning. In each layer of the language decoder it augments the public feedforward network (FFN) with a client-specific private FFN expert, with the goal to acquire domain-specific knowledge. However, independent expert training causes the private FFNs to learn representation of different scales and magnitudes, making merging the experts difficult. To reduce client-specific drift, we introduce a public-anchored expert composition stage that updates only routers and lightweight private projection adapters on a mix of local client data and public data, via an isotropic regularization loss, therefore making it cross-client rehearsal-free composition. During inference, DistMoE performs modular routing over public and private experts, enabling token-wise domain composition without explicit domain labels. Experiments across diverse visual-language benchmarks show that DistMoE enables flexible expert reuse, effective domain adaptation, and competitive performance while preserving modular control over client-specific knowledge.
  </details>

- **[ISRS-DETR: Detection-Guided Click Propagation for Remote Sensing Interactive Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02468)**  `arXiv:2608.02468`  `cs.CV`  
  _Thanh Duc Pham, Anh Nguyen, Duong Duc Hieu, Minh-Tan Pham_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive segmentation reduces the prohibitive cost of pixel-level annotation by allowing users to delineate objects with a few clicks. However, applying this paradigm directly to remote sensing imagery is non-trivial: ultra-high resolutions, small object sizes, and sparse spatial distributions all degrade segmentation quality. Recent work has addressed the resolution barrier and achieved competitive results in interactive segmentation for remote sensing (ISRS). However, they treat all instances of a class within an image as a single objective target. Consequently, interactions spent on one object contribute nothing to its same-class neighbours, and satisfactory masks may hinder the practicality of these frameworks. We observe that remote sensing scenes exhibit markedly strong inter-object correlation, meaning a single clicked object is highly informative about the rest of its category. Building on this, we propose ISRS-DETR, a detection-guided interactive segmentation framework that injects object-level evidence into both training and inference. Our ISRS-DETR employs a looped DETR-based decoder, a single weight-shared decoder layer iterated several times, with the interactive segmentation backbone to localise co-occurring same-class objects, and introduces a Dynamic Top-K Click Selection strategy that retains only reliable proposals and converts each into a simulated click, so one user interaction propagates across an entire class. Experiments on three standard remote sensing benchmarks show that ISRS-DETR achieves state-of-the-art accuracy while substantially reducing Number of Clicks (NoC). Code will be released for reproducibility upon acceptance.
  </details>

- **[AeroLLE: Nighttime Aerial Image Enhancement with Generated Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00702)**  `arXiv:2608.00702`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wei Lu, Hongyuan Liu, Liuqian Wang, Qi Ming, Xue Yang, Si-Bao Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Nighttime aerial image enhancement is constrained by the scarcity of aligned normal-light targets. Acquiring such targets from moving UAVs is difficult, and simple darkening of daytime images underrepresents the sensor noise, localized lighting, and uneven exposure of real nighttime captures. Generating normal-light references from real nighttime observations offers a practical way to fill this supervision gap. AeroNight pairs 1,200 real nighttime aerial RGB images with generated references selected through manual screening, preserving real imaging degradation in the inputs. This setting motivates AeroLLE, a lightweight two-stage framework whose context-aware Base Enhancer combines local processing with scene-level frequency information to recover visibility. Spatially Adaptive Exposure-Color Calibration (SAECC) then refines the base prediction through bounded low-resolution RGB gain and bias fields. AeroLLE achieves the highest PSNR and SSIM among the evaluated methods on both held-out test sets, reaching 17.62/17.89 dB PSNR and 0.5190/0.4964 SSIM on Test 1/Test 2, respectively. The model uses 255.24K parameters and 2.47G FLOPs, reducing FLOPs by 85.4\% relative to Multinex and improving PSNR by 1.56 dB on Test 2. AeroNight provides the first benchmark for pseudo-supervised nighttime aerial enhancement, and AeroLLE shows that generated references can support compact enhancement models.
  </details>

- **[OSEF: One-Step Evidence Fusion for Cross-Video Scene Procedure Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29401)**  `arXiv:2607.29401`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhentong Ye, Lei Zhang, Sijia Zhou, Yingda Yu, Yuehan Shi, Jiaqi Xuan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video Scene Procedure Planning (VSPP) predicts actions between supplied start and goal observations. We introduce Cross-Video Scene Procedure Planning (CVSPP), which instead takes a start-goal query and candidate videos, and predicts an action sequence together with its supporting video and interval. Two challenges arise: demonstrations of the same task can support different transitions, and an early retrieval error can deprive the planner of relevant evidence. We construct a dedicated benchmark from eleven video sources, with task-specific queries, candidate sets, and linked video-interval-plan references. Its fourteen settings support a broad study of nine adapted planner families. We propose One-Step Evidence Fusion (OSEF), which retains candidate features while producing interval-conditioned planning representations. Separate boundary scores model the supporting interval, and compatible-mass decoding combines the probability of the same complete plan across overlapping intervals before selecting the output. Joint evaluation requires video, interval, and plan to match one reference example. Across four matched development-data comparisons on NIV, CrossTask, and COIN, compatible-mass decoding increases mean joint success by 2.40-6.79 percentage points without retraining. On NIV, query-only controls retain plan accuracy yet sharply reduce joint success; donor-interface studies examine how VSPP models use retrieved visual states. We further identify temporal-reference sensitivity: changing only the reference projection can reverse the decoding gain, exposing an evaluation challenge for the new task.
  </details>

- **[MIBE: Multi-subject Interaction Benchmark and Evaluator for Personalized Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.01383)**  `arXiv:2607.01383`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhihan Chen, Yuhuan Zhao, Yijie Zhu, Xinyu Yao, Mengcong Ren, Suwen Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-subject personalized image generation aims to compose multiple distinct reference identities into a coherent scene while preserving their prompt-specified interactions. Existing generators frequently exhibit subject-binding failures as scene complexity scales: omitting requested entities (Existence), blending visual attributes across subjects (Appearance), or misassigning actions and spatial relations (Interaction). Compounding this modeling bottleneck, existing evaluation metrics, which are designed primarily for single-subject similarity or holistic image preference, align only weakly with human judgment on these coupled errors, and several degrade toward random agreement as the number of interacting subjects increases. To address this evaluation gap, we introduce the Multi-subject Interaction Benchmark and Evaluator (MIBE), a unified framework pairing a factorized benchmark (MIB) with a reference-conditioned evaluator (MIE). MIB systematically varies subject count, human-/object-heavy composition, and relation type (non-contact, occlusion, and physical interaction) within a decoupled two-tier data regime: MIB-Silver, a 60K-pair training corpus annotated via a rubric-guided dual-VLM protocol, and MIB-Gold, a 4,020-pair evaluation suite annotated under a double-blind human protocol across six state-of-the-art generators spanning both seen and held-out unseen architectures. Trained exclusively on MIB-Silver with a dual-head objective that jointly predicts pairwise preference rankings and facet-level diagnostics, MIE achieves 0.922 overall pairwise agreement with human consensus on MIB-Gold, including 0.982 on seen generators and 0.884 on held-out unseen generators. Using MIBE for cross-generator diagnosis, we quantify systematic binding bottlenecks across modern generators, providing reproducible benchmark infrastructure and interpretable supervision for future model alignment.
  </details>

- **[Pinpoint: Grounded Worldwide Image Geolocation via Cross-Source Retrieval and Reranking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04133)**  `arXiv:2606.04133`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nika Chuzhoy, Brian Hu, Amit A. Arora, Jae Ro, Sarthak S. Sahu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image geolocation aims to estimate where a photograph was taken from its visual content. At worldwide scale, this remains challenging because visual evidence is often ambiguous, diverse, and unevenly distributed. Prior work has typically treated geolocation of ordinary internet photos and street-view imagery as separate tasks, despite their complementary strengths: internet photos better match the appearance distribution of user-captured queries, while street-view imagery provides denser, geographically grounded coverage. We present Pinpoint, a retrieve-and-rerank architecture that combines both sources in a coarse-to-fine pipeline. A contrastive image-GPS embedder is trained on both user-uploaded Flickr photos and street-view imagery, learning a shared image-GPS embedding space that is used to retrieve candidate locations. An attention-based reranker then rescores retrieved candidates by combining candidate-level visual and GPS features with cross-source evidence from nearby locations to ground the prediction. Unlike recent prior work, Pinpoint does not rely on multimodal large-language models, making inference faster and more reproducible. Pinpoint achieves state-of-the-art results across all metrics on standard benchmarks for internet photos (IM2GPS3k and YFCC4k) and street-view imagery (OSV-5M).
  </details>

- **[How Far Has AI Come in Liver Fibrosis Staging? A Real-World Multi-Center Dataset and Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.25595)**  `arXiv:2605.25595`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuanye Liu, Nannan Shi, Zhejia Zhang, Hanxiao Zhang, Boya Wang, Derong Yu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite years of methodological progress, how far AI has come in liver fibrosis staging has never been systematically evaluated under the heterogeneous, multi-center conditions that define clinical practice. To address this gap, we introduce LiFS, a multi-center dataset and benchmark derived from the MICCAI 2025 CARE-Liver challenge, comprising 610 patients across multiple centers and scanners with multi-sequence. To the best of our knowledge, LiFS is the first benchmark providing complete gadoxetic acid-enhanced sequences with histopathology-confirmed annotations from diverse real-world scanners. Through systematic evaluation of nine independently developed LiFS methods under a common CARE 2025 setting, and comparison with in-cohort radiologist, we assess how far current AI has progressed toward clinically meaningful liver fibrosis staging from three complementary perspectives. First, against radiologists, the best AI methods approached the performance of the senior radiologist in selected settings and exceeded the junior radiologist on selected endpoints while median AI performance generally approached junior-radiologist levels. Second, from a data perspective, cross-center heterogeneity, label imbalance, and contrast-enhanced sequence variability emerge as the dominant challenges for AI methods. In particular, the hepatobiliary phase offers valuable functional information but also introduces cross-center variability, creating a practical dilemma in its use. Third, from a technical perspective, controlled experiments quantify the effects of several factors, providing practical evidence for model design in liver fibrosis staging. Overall, LiFS provides a rigorous real-world benchmark for positioning the current state of AI in liver fibrosis staging and for enabling future research on the key challenges that limit clinically reliable deployment.
  </details>

- **[CAB: Accelerating Flow and Diffusion Sampling via Rectification and Corrected Adams-Bashforth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16736)**  `arXiv:2605.16736`  `cs.CV`  
  _Anuska Roy, Pravin Nair_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Flow and diffusion models achieve high-fidelity, high-resolution image synthesis, but often require many function evaluations (NFEs) at sampling time. Existing acceleration methods either require additional training through distillation or rely on training-free high-order solvers, and both can degrade sample quality at low NFE budgets. We propose CAB (Corrected Adams-Bashforth), a training-free sampler that accelerates both flow and diffusion models. CAB first transforms the sampling dynamics to a common rectified coordinate system, and then applies a multistep Adams-Bashforth predictor augmented with a simple correction term based on past velocity evaluations and therefore incurs no additional NFEs. The resulting method is simple, has the same algorithmic form across model classes, and has at least third-order local truncation error and second-order global error. Experiments on pretrained flow and diffusion models, including class-conditional and large-scale text-to-image benchmarks, show that CAB improves quality-NFE trade-offs in the low-step regime of 6-20 NFEs. It also remains competitive with strong training-free samplers at higher step counts across most tested models. The official implementation is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[BARISTA: A Multi-Task Egocentric Benchmark for Compositional Visual Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.12074)**  `arXiv:2605.12074`  `cs.CV`  
  _Patrick Knab, Orgest Xhelili, Inis Buzi, Drago Andres Guggiana Nilo, Mohd Saquib Khan, Lorenz Kolb, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Scene understanding is central to general physical intelligence, and video is a primary modality for capturing both state and temporal dynamics of a scene. Yet understanding physical processes remains difficult, as models must combine object localization, hand-object interactions, relational parsing, temporal reasoning, and step-level procedural inference. Existing benchmarks usually evaluate these capabilities separately, limiting diagnosis of why models fail on procedural tasks. We introduce BARISTA, a densely annotated egocentric dataset and benchmark of 185 real-world coffee-preparation videos covering fully automatic, portafilter-based, and capsule-based workflows. BARISTA provides verified per-frame scene graphs linking persistent object identities to masks, tracks, boxes, attributes, typed relations, hand-object interactions, activities, and process steps. From these graphs, we derive zero-shot language-based tasks spanning phrase grounding, hand-object interaction recognition, referring, activity recognition, relation extraction, and temporal visual question answering. Experiments reveal strong variation across task families and no consistently dominant model family, positioning BARISTA as a challenging diagnostic benchmark for procedural video understanding. Code and dataset available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[GeoFocus: Blending Efficient Global-to-Local Perception for Multimodal Geometry Problem-Solving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.08524)**  `arXiv:2602.08524`  `cs.CV`  
  _Linger Deng, Yuliang Liu, Wenwen Yu, Zujia Zhang, Pei Fu, Zhenbo Luo, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Geometry problem-solving (GPS) remains challenging for large multimodal models, partly due to limitations in geometric perception. Existing perception-oriented approaches face two key limitations: code-based diagram reconstruction relies on verbose training targets, while perception question-answering supervision may overlook local geometric relations critical to reasoning. To address these limitations, we propose GeoFocus, a global-to-local perception training framework with two complementary components. VertexLang provides compact supervision of global diagram topology through vertex coordinates and connectivity, reducing annotation length by 88% and training time by 20% compared with code-based reconstruction. The Critical Local Perceptor generates targeted question-answer supervision across 13 categories of reasoning-relevant local geometric relations. On three GPS benchmarks, GeoFocus improves Qwen3-VL-2B/4B over matched GRPO-only baselines by 5.2/4.9 percentage points on average, without external tools or additional inference steps. A human evaluation of 200 problems further shows that GeoFocus reduces cases with geometric perception errors from 118 to 80. These results highlight perception-focused training as an effective complement to reasoning optimization for multimodal geometric problem-solving.
  </details>

- **[Towards progressive whole-slide image analysis with multi-scale pyramidal network, a pathologist inspired approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01951)**  `arXiv:2602.01951`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuyang Wu, Yifu Qiu, John-Melle Bokhorst, Ines P Nearchou, Sandrine Prost, Jonathan A Fallowfield, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pathologists make a low power assessment followed by limited but targeted higher power evaluation of regions of interest as the standard approach in gold-standard clinical histopathological reporting. Multiple-instance Learning (MIL) is commonly used for slide-level predictions in computational pathology and approaches using multi-scale features have been developed. However, existing multi-scale MIL methods typically rely on inflexible multi-magnification inputs and computationally expensive architectures. We therefore conceptualise a more efficient MIL method that is explicitly designed to mimic the efficient gold-standard human pathologist approach to multi-scale assessment. In this paper, we propose the Multi-scale Pyramidal Network (MSPN), a generalisable module for attention-based MIL. MSPN introduces progressive multi-scale whole-slide image analysis using only a single high-magnification input. It consists of (1) grid-based remapping that aggregates high-magnification features to derive spatially-aware coarse feature maps, and (2) the Coarse Guidance Network (CGN) that learns coarse contexts. We take MSPN as a generalisable module to 4 attention-based frameworks and benchmark on 11 clinically relevant tasks across 10 publicly available datasets with 2 foundation models. Our results demonstrate that by modelling the human pathologist paradigm in slide evaluation, MSPN consistently improves MIL across the compared configurations and is competitive on performance against 8 state-of-the-art multi-scale models while remaining lightweight and accessible in a manner that may increase implementability into under-resourced clinical systems.
  </details>

- **[On the Role of Rotation Equivariance in Monocular 2D-to-3D Human Pose Lifting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.13913)**  `arXiv:2601.13913`  `cs.CV`  
  _Pavlo Melnyk, Cuong Le, Urs Waldmann, Per-Erik Forssén, Bastian Wandt_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We consider monocular 3D human pose estimation (HPE), focusing on lifting detected 2D keypoints to 3D skeletal joints. Despite their success, we find that commonly used lifting models, including a state-of-the-art probabilistic method, exhibit substantial performance degradation under in-plane rotations. We systematically study SO(2) rotation equivariance and robustness across lifting models, comparing approaches that learn approximate equivariance via data augmentation with those that enforce exact equivariance via architectural constraints or pose canonicalisation. We investigate their effects on prediction accuracy, rotational robustness, cross-dataset generalisation, and computational efficiency. Using established HPE benchmarks, we show that rotation-based augmentation applied jointly to input and output poses substantially improves accuracy on rotated poses, reducing error by up to 72\% with little loss of original-pose accuracy. These improvements also extend to probabilistic lifting and real-world sequences containing natural full-body rotations. While augmented models can outperform specialised equivariant architectures at considerably lower inference cost, comparisons using canonicalisation-based versions of the same lifting models demonstrate that exact equivariance need not compromise prediction accuracy. Direct equivariance diagnostics further show that rotational robustness does not imply exact equivariance. Overall, the relative benefits of learning or enforcing equivariance depend on the lifting backbone and the mechanism used to achieve it.
  </details>

- **[EditThinker: Enhancing Image Editors via Iterative Reasoning with a Plug-and-Play VLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05965)**  `arXiv:2512.05965`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hongyu Li, Manyuan Zhang, Dian Zheng, Ziyu Guo, Yimeng Jia, Kaituo Feng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image editors can produce realistic images while still failing to satisfy the requested changes. Improving their instruction following requires identifying these failures and translating visual feedback into instructions that the editor can execute. We introduce EditThinker, an 8B vision-language model (VLM) that enhances existing image editors through iterative reasoning while keeping their weights fixed. EditThinker jointly critiques semantic consistency and visual quality, reasons about editing failures, and refines the instruction in a structured response. It interacts with the editor through a Critique-Refine-Repeat loop, providing a plug-and-play interface for feedback-driven editing. Learning effective refinements requires more than imitating plausible expert suggestions: the instructions must work with the capabilities of the underlying editor. We therefore combine supervised fine-tuning on expert demonstrations with reinforcement learning from executed editing outcomes, using format, critic, and edit rewards. To support this training, we construct ThinkEdit-167k, a dataset of 167k step-wise samples with unified supervision for critique, reasoning, and instruction refinement. Across four tested editors, EditThinker improves overall scores on GEdit-Bench-EN, ImgEdit-Bench, RISE-Bench, and KRIS-Bench. Ablations examine the joint model design, data allocation, and training across VLM backbones. In our GEdit round-budget study, refinement achieves higher scores with fewer editor calls than best-of-N selection. We will release our data construction framework, datasets, and models.
  </details>

- **[FlowLUT: Efficient Image Enhancement via Differentiable LUTs and Iterative Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.23608)**  `arXiv:2509.23608`  `cs.CV`  
  _Liubing Hu, Chen Wu, Anrui Wang, Dianjie Lu, Guijuan Zhang, Zhuoran Zheng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep learning-based image enhancement methods face a fundamental trade-off between computational efficiency and representational capacity. For example, although a conventional three-dimensional Look-Up Table (3D LUT) can process a degraded image in real time, it lacks representational flexibility and depends solely on a fixed prior. To address this problem, we introduce FlowLUT, a novel end-to-end model that integrates the efficiency of LUTs, multiple priors, and the parameter-independent characteristic of flow-matched reconstructed images. Specifically, firstly, the input image is transformed in color space by a collection of differentiable 3D LUTs (containing a large number of 3D LUTs with different priors). Subsequently, a lightweight content-aware dynamically predicts fusion weights, enabling scene-adaptive color correction with $\mathcal{O}(1)$ complexity. Next, a lightweight fusion prediction network runs on multiple 3D LUTs, with $\mathcal{O}(1)$ complexity for scene-adaptive colorthis http URL, to address the inherent representation limitations of LUTs, we design an innovative iterative flow matching method to restore local structural details and eliminate artifacts. Finally, the entire model is jointly optimized under a composite loss function enforcing perceptual and structural fidelity. Extensive experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of our method on three benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[Concepts in Motion: Temporal Concept Bottleneck Model for Interpretable Video Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.20899)**  `arXiv:2509.20899`  `cs.CV`  
  _Patrick Knab, Sascha Marton, Philipp J. Schubert, Drago Guggiana, Christian Bartelt_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Concept Bottleneck Models (CBMs) enable interpretable image classification by structuring predictions around human-understandable concepts, but extending this paradigm to video remains challenging due to the difficulty of extracting concepts and modeling them over time. In this paper, we introduce MoTIF (Moving Temporal Interpretable Framework), a transformer-based concept architecture that operates on sequences of temporally grounded concept activations, by employing per-concept temporal self-attention to model when individual concepts recur and how their temporal patterns contribute to predictions. Central to the framework is a class-conditioned VLM-based concept discovery module that extracts object- and action-centric textual concepts from training videos, yielding temporally expressive concept sets without manual concept annotation. Across multiple video benchmarks, this combination improves over global concept bottlenecks and remains competitive within the interpretable concept-bottleneck setting, while narrowing the gap to strong black-box video baselines that we report as contextual references. Code available atthis http URL.
  </details>

- **[ZIP: Scalable Crowd Counting via Zero-Inflated Poisson Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.19955)**  `arXiv:2506.19955`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yiming Ma, Victor Sanchez, Tanaya Guha_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The majority of crowd counting methods directly regress blockwise density maps using losses based on Mean Squared Error (MSE). This practice has two critical limitations: 1) it fails to account for the extreme spatial sparsity of annotations (i.e., over 95% of standard $8\times8$ blocks are empty across most benchmarks), which makes supervision signals in informative regions diluted by the predominant zeros. 2) MSE corresponds to a Gaussian error model that poorly matches discrete, non-negative count data. We introduce a novel crowd counting framework that is scalable and directly addresses the above limitations. This framework, named ZIP, models blockwise counts with a Zero-Inflated Poisson likelihood: A zero-inflation term models excess zeros in sparse annotations, while the Poisson component captures expected counts while respecting their discrete, non-negative nature. We provide a generalization analysis showing that the ZIP negative log-likelihood (NLL) has the same order of uniform generalization gap as Poisson NLL while offering a richer zero-inflated likelihood family, and that its bound scales more favorably with the number of spatial blocks than optimal-transport-based supervision. To assess the scalability and broad applicability of ZIP, we apply the same formulation across diverse backbones, from lightweight models to high-capacity architectures, spanning more than $100\times$ in both parameter count and inference FLOPs. Experiments on four standard crowd-counting benchmarks show that ZIP achieves state-of-the-art or highly competitive performance across a wide range of backbone capacities. Although ZIP is designed for sparse blockwise count maps, it also remains effective in dense crowd scenes, reducing MAE and RMSE by 3.9% and 8.5%, respectively, over mPrompt on UCF-QNRF. Code and trained weights are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Revisiting Out-of-Distribution Detection in Semantic Difference Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.11254)**  `arXiv:2411.11254`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xingming Long, Jie Zhang, Shiguang Shan, Xilin Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The primary goal of most existing out-of-distribution (OOD) detection works is to identify inputs with "Semantic Shifts". However, the existing definition of "Semantic Shift" in previous studies remains vague, resulting in controversial choices of OOD samples in many benchmarks. Consequently, some benchmarks are too trivial for evaluating OOD detection, whereas others include OOD instances that are practically undetectable. In this paper, we propose a formal definition of "Semantic Difference Space", which reflects the scope of a model's OOD detection capability after training convergence. The definition is grounded in an analysis of how the loss-based optimization shapes the model's perceptual toward OOD inputs, offering theoretical explanations for the controversies regarding the selection of OOD samples across existing benchmarks. To validate the correctness of our analysis, we construct multiple datasets, primarily in the context of image classification, and we find that compared to different OOD detection methods, different OOD settings play a more decisive role in the detection results. Finally, we propose a simple yet effective OOD Separability Probe (OSP) method to evaluate whether the existing OOD detection benchmarks are reasonable, identifying issues in some benchmarks and highlighting a few that are worth further exploration.
  </details>

- **[Retinexformer: One-stage Retinex-based Transformer for Low-light Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.06705)**  `arXiv:2303.06705`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuanhao Cai, Hao Bian, Jing Lin, Haoqian Wang, Radu Timofte, Yulun Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  When enhancing low-light images, many deep learning algorithms are based on the Retinex theory. However, the Retinex model does not consider the corruptions hidden in the dark or introduced by the light-up process. Besides, these methods usually require a tedious multi-stage training pipeline and rely on convolutional neural networks, showing limitations in capturing long-range dependencies. In this paper, we formulate a simple yet principled One-stage Retinex-based Framework (ORF). ORF first estimates the illumination information to light up the low-light image and then restores the corruption to produce the enhanced image. We design an Illumination-Guided Transformer (IGT) that utilizes illumination representations to direct the modeling of non-local interactions of regions with different lighting conditions. By plugging IGT into ORF, we obtain our algorithm, Retinexformer. Comprehensive quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that our Retinexformer significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods on thirteen benchmarks. The user study and application on low-light object detection also reveal the latent practical values of our method. Code, models, and results are available atthis https URL. Our project page atthis https URLshows more visual results. An online Gradio demo is available on Hugging Face atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Unlocking the Power of Loops in Pretrained LLMs with Loop Residuals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31094)**  `arXiv:2611.31094`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Xuanlei Zhao, Hailun Xu, Kai Wang, Yang Luo, Yingxuan Ren, Zirui Zhu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Looped Transformers iterate a shared block of layers to increase computation without adding parameters, offering stronger performance and test-time scaling. However, this power has so far required training from scratch and yielded only modest gains for LLMs. Finetuning existing models into loops is far cheaper, yet also brings only marginal gains over SFT. We propose LoopRes, an effective, scalable, and simple approach that turns loops into significant and consistent gains for pretrained LLMs. Building on Attention Residuals, LoopRes lets each looped layer attend directly to the outputs of earlier loops. Since these outputs are iterates of the same layers and become harder to tell apart, we give each a trainable loop-aware key that encodes its structural identity. Attending to all earlier loops also lets layers skip intermediate ones, so we restrict each layer to a residual window of the latest W loops, forcing every loop to build incrementally on recent ones. Across 6 pretrained LLMs from 3 model families and 9 benchmarks, LoopRes delivers significant and consistent gains: at 2x compute, it improves Qwen3-1.7B-Base by 29.1% over SFT, more than doubling the gain of the state-of-the-art baseline. The gains hold on larger models up to 30B and across all three families. More importantly, from 1.7B to 8B, LoopRes scales more steeply than SFT as compute grows, reaching a higher ceiling while needing less compute on long outputs. These results establish LoopRes as an effective looping approach, delivering both better performance and better test-time scaling.
  </details>

- **[When Should Decision Models Compare Candidates?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20335)**  `arXiv:2611.20335`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Fangzhi Li, Quan Shi, Wenting Yang, Yueqiao Chen, Jiayu Chang, Shuting Zhao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Language-model agents frequently select among candidate actions, such as tools and web elements. Should decision models score candidates independently or allow their scores to depend on other candidates? We investigate this question by comparing three approaches: Pointwise scoring, Setwise scoring, and Candidate Attention. Across five agent-decision benchmarks, Pointwise scoring remains competitive, while Setwise scoring performs better on some tool-selection and binary-decision tasks. Candidate Attention does not consistently improve upon Pointwise scoring, despite allowing cross-candidate interaction. Using two controlled synthetic tasks, we further examine whether models learn to use candidate-set information. With targeted relational training, Setwise models learn candidate dependencies and generalize to shifted inputs, while Candidate Attention shows weaker transfer. Our results show that access to other candidates does not guarantee effective comparison, and that its benefits depend on both task requirements and training.
  </details>

- **[Write Facts the Way Models Read Them: Lifting the Two-Hop Curse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01519)**  `arXiv:2611.01519`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Zhiyun Shi_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A language model that learns a new fact by fine-tuning can repeat it but cannot reason with it: taught that a person is a citizen of a new country, it still cannot say, without chain-of-thought, who the head of state of that person's country is. This two-hop curse is widely treated as a reasoning deficit, and the strongest knowledge editors answer it with synthesized reasoning data. We show that the reasoning is intact and the writing is wrong. Fine-tuning writes a new fact as an answer to be said, whereas the second hop reads the bridge ("that person's country") as an entity, from the middle layers on: patching the model's own representation of the entity into the bridge at those layers largely restores the second hop, yet after fine-tuning the bridge there is no closer to the new entity than before the edit. We therefore propose read-equivalent writing (Legible): one loss term makes the bridge phrase, at the layer where the second hop finishes reading it, match the model's own reading of the new entity, and keeps the same relation's other subjects unchanged. Phrases and targets come from the edited model itself, and no multi-hop training data is used. Across Qwen2.5, Llama-3 and Qwen3, Legible (I) composes learned facts more often than the model composes its own knowledge, on MQuAKE's two-hop-curse questions; (II) reaches 1.8-2.6x the bridge accuracy of CaKE, whose reasoning data composes no better than plain fine-tuning, and has the best average over composition, accuracy and locality against four families of writers; and (III) lifts the curse on RippleEdits, on the benchmark of Balesni et al., who named the curse, and at 14B.
  </details>

- **[RheoSampling: Resolving the One-Hot Dilemma in Stochastic Dynamic-Tree Speculative Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21827)**  `arXiv:2609.21827`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Qiao Hu, Yepeng Weng, Bo Zhang, Takehisa Yairi_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Speculative decoding accelerates LLM inference by drafting multiple tokens in parallel, with tree-based methods further improving efficiency through hierarchical structures. Dynamic-tree methods such as EAGLE-3 perform well under greedy decoding via deterministic top-K expansion and global pruning. However, in stochastic decoding (T>0), this mechanism collapses the draft distribution into one-hot probabilities, causing a severe drop in acceptance rate. This creates a dilemma: dynamic-tree methods sacrifice stochastic sampling to preserve context-aware topology, while static-tree methods preserve stochastic sampling with context-agnostic structures. The issue arises because the same probability distribution is used for two conflicting tasks: constructing the tree and verifying tokens. This coupling makes direct injection of randomness challenging due to the resulting stochastic process. We resolve this by decoupling these roles: RheoSampling assigns a token sampled from the draft distribution a proxy probability for tree expansion and pruning alongside its true sampling probability for verification. Specifically, we inject a sampled token among the deterministic top-K slots and treat it with different probabilities during construction and verification, making RheoSampling the first dynamic-tree method with both context-aware top-K construction and stochastic sampling while maintaining losslessness. We establish the lossless guarantee through an equivalence-class analysis that compresses the stochastic tree space into tractable classes. An OT-based verification strategy and a sparse draft mechanism ensure that theoretical gains translate into practical efficiency. Experiments across LLMs and benchmarks demonstrate improvements in acceptance rate and speedup over state-of-the-art dynamic tree methods. This framework may provide a template for analyzing stochastic tree structures.
  </details>

- **[The Imperfective Paradox Is Not Necessarily in Large Language Models: A Benchmark Failure Before a Model Failure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25005)**  `arXiv:2608.25005`  `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Kaiqiao Han, Mark Steedman, Yizhou Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent work uses an NLI benchmark of the imperfective paradox to argue that large language models exhibit a Teleological Bias and Calibration Crisis. We identify three validity concerns in benchmark construction, including aspectual reduction, semantic bias, and relation bias. Under a strict NLI standard, 76% of the Group A instances do not explicitly rule out culmination; in native-speaker annotation, 38% of Group A and 29% of Group C examples permit an alternative interpretation. To better isolate the semantic contrast, we construct Lexically Matched Minimal Pairs. Moreover, by formulating event-semantic NLI as a multi-step reasoning problem, we evaluate intermediate semantic judgments and use oracle-guided settings. We find that incorrect NLI entailment does not fully support teleological bias. Prompting often changes final NLI decisions without reliably improving the underlying semantic judgments, offering a possible explanation for the Calibration Crisis.
  </details>

- **[ArEssays: Towards Robust Arabic Handwritten Text Understanding in Educational Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.32211)**  `arXiv:2611.32211`  `cs.CL` `cs.CV`  
  _Hamdy Mubarak, Anas Madkoor, Ehsaneddin Asgari, Majd Hawasly_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automated understanding of handwritten Arabic student essays remains challenging due to script complexity and high variabilityin children's writing. We introduce ArEssays, a large-scale dataset of authentic essays from primary to secondary studentsacross Egypt, with both verbatim transcriptions and linguistically corrected versions. We benchmark 34 vision-language and OCRmodels, including fine-tuned systems, and find large performance gaps on real student handwriting. We release the dataset,training pipelines, and results to support research in Arabic OCR, document understanding, and educational NLP.
  </details>

- **[Agent Collectives Should Not Detect Their Own Imposters: A Chess Case Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.09027)**  `arXiv:2605.09027`  `cs.CL` `cs.AI` `cs.LG` `cs.MA`
